資料中心監管風暴衝擊 AI 供應鏈，美股全面重挫
2026 年 11 月 06 日
一、 市場動態
1. 資料中心監管疑慮引爆 AI 供應鏈賣壓，美股選後漲勢一日反轉：民主黨取得參、眾兩
院多數，美國進入分裂政府格局。S&P 500 於 11 月 4 日先漲 1.58%，11 月 5 日卻重
挫 4.36%，創2025 年 4 月以來最大單日跌幅 ；Nasdaq 跌 5.52%，費半大跌 12.25%，
十一大產業全數收黑。主因有三：一是資料中心開發成為州級政策議題，德州州長當
選人主張暫停新建，全美新增容量約三成可能受影響；二是高通與 Arm 財報不如預

期；三是建設遞延將推高依賴外部融資業者的持有成本。費半重挫前已自 6 月高點回
落近三成，政策不確定性進一步放大跌幅（詳見表一）。
表一：美股主要指數表現（2026/11/05 收盤）
主要指數 收盤點位 單日漲跌幅
S&P 500 指數 7,353.73 −4.36%
Nasdaq 綜合指數 24,315.00 −5.52%
費城半導體指數 9,011.14 −12.25%
資料來源：Reuters、Nasdaq、元大銀行整理
2. 波動率指數急升，公債殖利率全面走低，油價反彈：11 月 5 日避險情緒升溫，VIX 盤
中升至 28.09（前日 16.61）。資金湧入美債，2 年、5 年、10 年及 30 年期殖利率分
別降至 4.46%、4.59%、4.84%及 5.25%，呈牛市陡峭化；12 月升息一碼機率由約
83%降至約 65%。布蘭特原油反彈 3.88%至每桶 98.29 美元，通膨前景仍具不確定性
（詳見表二） 。
表二：美債殖利率與避險資產變化（2026/11/05）

2 年期公債殖利率 4.46% −12 bps
10 年期公債殖利率 4.84% −6 bps
VIX 波動率指數（盤中高點） 28.09 前日收盤 16.61
布蘭特原油期貨（美元／桶） 98.29 +3.88%
12 月升息一碼機率（CME） 約 65% 前日約 83%
資料來源：Bloomberg、WSJ／CME、元大銀行整理
二、 下跌原因
1. 民主黨橫掃國會及州長選舉，資料中心開發成為州級政策議題：德州州長當選人 Gina
Hinojosa 主張暫停新建資料中心，俄亥俄州與喬治亞州當選人也提出限制。地方政府

### Page 2

確性不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決
策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
可透過電價分攤、建設許可及稅務優惠影響新案進度，UBS 估計 2028 年全美新增資
料中心容量約三成可能受影響。國會對科技巨頭與資料中心用電的監督亦可能增強，
半導體、電力設備及資料中心類股成為賣壓重心。
2. 高通手機晶片業績低於預期，加重半導體類股賣壓：高通財年第四季調整後 EPS 年減
28%至 2.17 美元，手機晶片營收年減 26%至 51.37 億美元，低於預期，主因蘋果相
關收入減少；汽車與物聯網營收雖年增 36%，仍難抵銷缺口，股價隔日收跌 9.5%，
進一步放大半導體跌幅。
3. 融資成本與投資回收壓力加重，依賴外部融資業者首當其衝：資料中心資本支出多發

生於上線前，若電力、許可或工程延後，收入認列隨之推遲，利息與持有成本卻須持
續負擔。儘管 12 月升息機率降至約 65%，借款成本仍高，依賴外部融資或單一客戶
合約的 AI 基礎建設業者將面臨回報壓縮與再融資風險。
4. 科技股在本次重挫前已明顯修正 ， 評價落至極低水準：費半自 6 月 22 日高點 14,634.72
點已回落逾 30%，Nasdaq 亦自 10 月 6 日高點 27,599.89 點回落逾 10%。市場質疑
AI 投資效益，降低對高評價企業的容忍度，政策不確定性因而放大修正。近期跌幅較
屬估值下調而非基本面惡化，若晶片訂單與獲利預估維持穩定，市場有望回溫。
表三：11/5 主要科技股及類股表現
標的 單日漲跌幅 重點說明
費城半導體指數 −12.25% 選後跌幅最深，AI 供應鏈評價重估
高通（Qualcomm） −9.5% 手機晶片營收年減 26%，低於預期
軟體類股 ETF（IGV） −4.48% 跌幅明顯小於半導體
資料來源：Reuters、Bloomberg、元大銀行整理

三、 市場看法
1. 短期評價修正而非長期趨勢反轉，AI 資本循環仍處擴張階段：我們視本波為多頭格局
中的健康修正。修正反映股東對龐大資本承諾要求更高風險補償，而非 AI 變現失速：
Google Cloud 第三季營收年增 103%、 營業利益年增209%，四大雲端業者資本支出
預估上修至約 1.16 兆美元，顯示企業看見更大市場而主動加碼（詳見表四）。此外，
政策衝擊集中於州與地方，聯邦仍支持資料中心擴建，選舉結果本質是要求用電成本
內部化，而非否定 AI 投資。
表四：AI 基本面持續擴張──關鍵指標一覽
指標 最新數據 解讀
Google Cloud 第三季營收 年增 103% AI 需求快速轉化為收入
Google Cloud 第三季營業利益 年增 209% 獲利能力未因擴張而惡化
四大雲端業者資本支出預估 約 8,732 億→1.16 兆美元 企業主動加碼，投資循環仍擴張
資料來源：元大投顧、Bloomberg；元大銀行整理

策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。 
2. 留意五大反彈訊號，利空逐步消化後市場可望回穩：（一）州級電價與併網制度若於
2027 年上半年 建立 ，延後 專案可望 下半年重新加速 ，眾議院 以 417 比 3 通過
《Ratepayer Protection Act》亦顯示跨黨派共識；（二）雲端業者資本支出指引未下
修、未見取消訂單；（三）11 月 10 日 CPI 及 13 日 PPI 物價月增率回落；（四）財政
部維持附息債標售規模，資金回流市場；（五）VIX 回落且投資等級債利差穩定。訊號
出現後，市場有望回歸基本面。 
3. 政策先影響建設速度，AI 需求仍須以訂單驗證：州級限制首先改變的是資料中心選址
與交付時程，而非 AI 需求本身。雲端客戶若維持支出計畫，專案可調整順序或轉往他
州，惟設備交付遞延將壓抑供應商近季營收；聯邦立法限制則須經國會及總統否決權，
短期落地難度高。若主要客戶訂單穩定，本次修正較屬建設時程遞延的風險重定價。 
4. 新增算力面臨遞延，既有算力相對有利：高盛估計可能收緊開發限制的州約占美國規
劃中資料中心容量的 45%，其中德州約占 25%。新案放慢下，大型雲端業者既有算力
可望受惠於利用率與議價能力提升，資本支出延後亦減輕現金流壓力；仍待併網的專
案及高槓桿業者則面臨回收遞延，產業鏈分化將擴大。 
5. 歷史經驗顯示選後股市多正向，後續表現仍宜留意基本面變化：彭博統計 1950 年以
來，美國期中選舉後 12 個月 S&P 500 平均報酬約 15%，19 次全數收正，最高為 1954
年的逾 30%，最弱的 1986 年亦小幅上漲。府會分治抑制政策大幅轉向為選後行情主
要支撐，惟後續表現仍須觀察基本面(詳見下圖)。

6. 企業獲利仍強勁成長，本次修正以估值與風險溢價重估為主：彭博 11 月 4 日統計，
S&P 500 已公布財報公司中 79.85%優於預期，資訊科技更達 92.45%，第三季 EPS
成長率分別為 29.0%及 67.8%（詳見表五）。財報為美股提供中期支撐，短期評價則
取決於 2027 年獲利預估能否承受建設遞延與融資成本壓力。 
表五：S&P 500 第三季財報統計（截至 2026/11/04） 
類別 已公布家數 優於預期家數 優於預期比率 第三季 EPS 成長率 
S&P 500 397 317 79.85% 29.0%

資料來源：Bloomberg；元大銀行整理
7. 最新經濟數據仍具韌性，聯準會利率路徑仍具變數：初領失業救濟金降至 18.9 萬人，
第三季勞動生產力季增年率 3.6%、單位勞動成本季減 0.7%，經濟仍具韌性。惟油價
逼近每桶 100 美元，若通膨高於預期，升息定價恐再升溫，壓抑科技股估值與長債價
格。
8. 信用市場是判斷風險是否由股權評價擴散至金融條件的重要指標：若 AI 企業持續擴大
資本支出與外部融資，償債能力將受關注。大型投資等級科技企業現金流穩定，較能

吸收壓力；高槓桿或依賴單一客戶的 AI 基礎建設業者則較易受利差上升衝擊。信用利
差若維持穩定，風險仍以股權評價調整為主；若同步擴大，則須警惕金融條件收緊。
四、 投資建議：
1. 短期修正不改 AI 長期趨勢 ， 切勿恐慌殺低錯殺優質標的：本次重挫源自選後政策不確
定性與評價重新定價，AI 需求與企業獲利尚未反轉，屬多頭中的短期波動。應避免恐
慌出清而錯殺優質標的；已持有者以續抱為主，加碼宜分批逢低，避免高槓桿操作。
2. 以股債平衡穿越短期波動，等待基本面重新主導市場：我們認為，本次修正屬短期政
策衝擊與評價調整，中長期科技股展望仍正面。在政策與通膨明朗前，建議將集中度
過高的科技股部位，適度轉向波動較低的平衡型或多重資產商品，搭配優質債券穩定
收益，並保留股票部位分批布局優質標的，待反彈訊號確認後再提高風險資產比重。
3. 股票部位聚焦優質、評價回落且前景較佳的產業：修正後部分優質企業評價已回到合
理區間，股票建議聚焦：一、握有長期電力合約、已通電園區的大型雲端業者；二、
明年展望佳的 AI 供應鏈，如 ASIC、光通訊、電力設備、半導體設備及資安；三、跌

幅較小的軟體及非科技優質股。高度仰賴新建資料中心與外部融資的業者，宜待環境
明朗再評估。
4. 中天期美國公債及高信評投資等級債仍較具優勢：在美國政策風險升溫及科技股評價
調整期間，美國公債仍具投資組合避險功能，但考量通膨與能源價格的不確定性，現
階段不建議延長存續期間。中天期美國公債及高信評投資等級債在兼顧票息收益、利
率敏感度與資產配置穩定性上仍較具優勢。信用債方面，營運現金流充足、槓桿適中
及再融資需求可控的發行人仍較為穩健，對AI 相關企業則須進一步評估資本支出、合
約現金流與債務到期結構。

聯發科 (2454 TT) 
ASIC 業務引領營運成長

永豐觀點 
ASIC 業務營收規模優於預期，2028 年還有第二個 ASIC 客戶步入量產(屬於 AI 機
櫃設計等級，公司能承接的工作範圍更多)，帶動營運顯著成長。 
 投資評價與建議 
維持買進：ASIC 業務貢獻優於預期，研究部上修獲利，預估聯發科 2026 年稅後
EPS 72.8 元(原預估 70.5 元)，2027 年稅後 EPS 142.78 元(原預估 132.52 元)，
2028 年稅後 EPS 321.22 元(原預估 312.93 元)。聯發科 ASIC 業務進展優於預期，
可望帶動後續營運強勁成長，研究部對聯發科維持買進，目標價由5570 元調升至
5800 元(由 2027~2028 年 EPS 均值與 25 倍評價)。

ESG 評析 
聯發科企業永續評鑑整體為 A 等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向
平均得分分別為 A 等與 A 等。 
SinoPac+ ESG 評鑑系統評等

資料來源：SinoPac+ ESG 評等 (依循 2025 SinoPac+企業永續評鑑方法學) 
註 1：資誠永續發展服務股份有限公司僅於ＥＳＧ評鑑系統方法學建置過程中，就評分指標提供
專業意見，對於評分結果及評估報告內容之完整性及真實性，不負擔保責任，亦不對閱讀或使用
本評估報告之第三方負任何責任。 
註 2：永豐投顧已獲 SASB 授權，於研究中使用 SASB 重大性地圖之一般議題分類及揭露主題。

### Page 2

2

聯發科為 台系 IC 設計龍頭廠商 ：聯發科為全球第五大 IC 設計業者 (僅次於
Qualcomm、Broadcom、nVidia)，著墨智慧型手機、智慧裝置與電源管理晶片的
發展 。 在智慧型手機晶片方面的主要競爭對手為Qualcomm、 展訊及華為海思等 ，
在智慧家庭、AIoT 等應用領域則以 Broadcom、Marvell、聯詠及瑞昱等廠商為
主。客戶主要透過代理商供應產品，終端客戶以全球手機品牌(OPPO、VIVO、小
米、Samsung、榮耀等)以及智慧家電品牌為主。 
3Q26 財報優於預期 ：聯發科 3Q26 營收 1680.58 億元(+10%QoQ，+18%YoY)，
毛利率 46.3%， 營業利益率16.1%， 稅後淨利278.82 億元(+15%QoQ，+11%YoY)，
稅後 EPS 17.37 元，財報優於預期，主要是營收超標、營業費用控管得宜所致。 
聯發科 3Q26 財報各個細項表現如下：(1) 營收季增 10%，超逾目標值 1522 億元
~1598 億元(QoQ+0%~+5%)，三大產品線表現均優於預期，得益於專案挹注程
度與供應鏈產能爭取進度較佳，三大產品線以Smart Edge Platform 挹注最顯著。
聯發科 3Q26 三 大 產 品 線 營 運 動 能 如 下 ：Smart edge platform 季增
20%(+68%YoY， 營收佔比58%)、 電源晶片(+1%QoQ，+18%YoY， 營收佔比5%)、
手機營收滑落 1%QoQ(-17%YoY，營收佔比 37%)，其中 Smart edge platform
季增幅度超逾公司原預期的，QoQ+5%~+9%，公司前次法說即已表示將有多項
聯網與車載項目於 3Q26 進入量產 ， 現在看來5G modem/AI tablet(天璣 9500 導
入三星平板電腦 Tab S120/AI PC / 車載晶片貢獻更優，並且 TV SoC 內含 DRAM
漲價亦有貢獻。 電源晶片亦優於公司原預期的持平， 主要是來自運算與資料中心市
占率提升。手機僅季減1%，落於公司原預期的 QoQ-5%~+0%的上緣，應受惠於
旗艦晶片天璣 9600 出台所致。(2) 毛利率 46.3%，與前季相仿，落於目標值區間
44.5%~47.5%區間。(3) 營業費用 507 億元(+7%QoQ)，營業費用成長性<營收
成長性，反映營業費用控管得宜。(4) 聯發科 3Q26 存貨 1535 億元(+56%QoQ)，
庫存天數由 2Q26 的 102 天增至 127 天，主要是因應 ASIC 業務拓展。

### Page 3

4Q26 營收展望優於預期：聯發科預估 4Q26 營收 2101 億元~2218 億元(以美元
對新台幣匯率 31.7 元計算)，QoQ+25%~+32%，YoY+40% ~+48%，毛利率目
標值區間下滑 1%至 43.5%~46.5%，營業費用率 24%~28%，反映營業利益率
16%~23%，優於過往三年多落於 14%~19%水準。聯發科 4Q26 展望細項如下：
(1) 4Q26 營運由 ASIC 業務驅動，(2) 4Q26 毛利率目標值區間下滑 1%，主要是受
ASIC 業務毛利率<公司均值，以及晶片價格因製造成本上升而出現轉嫁事宜的綜
合因素影響。(3) ASIC 業務的營業利益率>公司均值，故帶動公司 4Q26 營業利益
率回升。(4) 公司預期 2026 年營收成長性，超逾原預期的 YoY+5%~+9%。研究
部預估聯發科 4Q26 營收 2180 億元(＋30%QoQ，+45%YoY)，毛利率 45%，稅
後獲利 405.2 億元(+45%QoQ，+77%YoY)，稅後 EPS 25.25 元。 
ASIC 業務貢獻值優於預期：聯發科本次法說揭示 ASIC 業務貢獻值優於預期：(1) 
公司預期 2026 年 ASIC 業務 25 億美元(優於前次法說僅預期 >20 億美元)，
Zebrafish 已在 09/2026 開始小量出貨。(2) 2027 年 ASIC 業務 160 億美元(市佔
率 20%)，落於公司原預期的市占率 15%~20%與貢獻 120 億~160 億美元的上
緣。(3) 第二款 ASIC Humufish 維持預計將於 1Q28 步入量產，目前該平台部分
die 業已 tape out 完成，主要 die 也在 tape out 進行當中，先進封裝仍將優先採
用 Intel EMIB 。(4) 2027 年的營業利益率將>20%，反映營運槓桿效益提升。(5) 
第三代 ASIC(若順利應於 2029~2030 貢獻)則目前尚未定義最終規格。 
再者，公司亦表示第二個 ASIC 業務(搭載 NVLink Fusion) 預計 2028 年開始出貨
(前季法說僅表示尚與潛在客戶洽談中，本季已明確表示將於 2028 年出貨)，此為
新客戶(公司表示非中國客戶，故應表示該客戶非先前傳言的 Bytedance)。協助單
顆晶片設計與進入 NVLink Fusion 機櫃級設計的差異，在於幫客戶將晶片納入整
合進 Nvidia 的 AI 機櫃系統，需要增加高速互聯/相容性/記憶體架構/封裝協同設
計，以與 Nvidia 的交換器或網路系統偕同運作，反映聯發科能承接的工作範圍更
多，亦即接到單一客戶的商業價值應該更多。但因為此為聯發科首次導入 AI 機櫃
等級的業務，相關客戶與細項內容，均尚難知曉，故研究部本次財務預估尚未將此
估算在內。 
維持買進：考量 ASIC 業務貢獻優於預期，研究部上修獲利，預估聯發科 2026 年
營收 6874 億元(+15%YoY)，毛利率 45.87%，營業利率 16.9%，稅後獲利 1168
億元(+11%YoY)，稅後 EPS 72.8 元(原預估 70.5 元)，2027 年營收 1.17 兆元
(+71%YoY)， 毛利率43.77%， 營業利率21.5%， 稅後獲利2292 億元(+96%YoY)，
圖一：聯發科庫存天數與營收走勢

### Page 8

Yageo (2327.TW)
30 October 2026 Citi Research
8
Yageo
Company description
Established in 1977 and listed in 1993, Yageo is a leading supplier of passive
electronic components based in Taiwan. The company offers a wide array of
products including MLCC, chip resistors, magnetics, tantalum, and other
components, aiming to be the total solution provider for its customers. By
capacity, the company currently ranks No. 1 in chip resistor and tantalum and

No. 3 in MLCC and inductor globally. Key customers of Yageo include leading
EMS, ODMs, and distributors. The company also has 29 sales offices, 51
manufacturing sites, and 20 R&D centers in 29 nations worldwide.
Investment strategy
We have a Buy rating on Yageo shares. Yageo is widely known as a passive
electronic component supplier. Although passive components are generally
viewed as a commodity, Yageo is no longer a commodity play, in our view,

given the company’s foray into premium markets through M&As, which
should help mitigate the impact of sector cyclicality and order volatility. We
expect a more diversified product portfolio to drive stable earnings and better
margins, while improving utilization in its commodity component business
following the end of inventory digestions should add additional fuel.
Valuation
We set our target price at NT$1,000, based on 23x average 2027E-28E
earnings. We value Yageo based on PE as we believe the company’s earnings

performance and industry dynamics would be the key focuses of the market.
Our target multiple of 23x is at the higher end of the trading range since 2022
but in line with global peers average. During this period the company has gone
through the cycles, successfully integrated the acquired business to enhance
its product offerings, and started to deliver stable margins and earnings.
Risks
Key downside risks to our target price and rating include: 1) slower-than-

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be

published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

Industry View In-Line
Results:
• AT&S (ATSV.VI, Not Covered) reported C3Q revenue of €682mn (+24% q/q):
Electronics Solutions (+26% q/q) and Microelectronics (ABF; +23% q/q).
• C3Q/F2Q Microelectronics (ABF) EBIT margin increased another 580bp q/q,
to 30.5%, which management attributed to higher volumes, better product

mix and an improved pricing environment.
Guidance:
• Revenue: +45-55% y/y, unchanged.
• EBITDA margin: +32-37%, unchanged.
• AT&S expects its ongoing capacity expansion for the Microelectronics
business to have a positive impact on revenue and earnings already in the
current fiscal year.
Capacity expansion:
• In June (link), AT&S announced the expansion of its production site in Kulim,
based on agreements with its customer AMD (covered by Joseph Moore) and

another leading technology company.
• AT&S reaffirmed that the planned investments of €1.5–2.0bn, supported by
long-term customer commitments, will provide additional capacity to meet
growing demand for advanced IC substrates.
• In September (link), AT&S announced an expanded collaboration with
Marvell (covered by Joseph Moore) to further increase advanced IC substrate
capacity for next-generation AI and cloud infrastructure as well. Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
Microelectronics EBIT margin up another 580bp q/q, to 30.5%, partly from
pricing; positive read for ABF peers Unimicron and NYPCB.
Customers'  commitments underpin €1.5-2.0bn Kulim expansion. Signals tight
supply, multi-year visibility.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Unimicron (3037.TW)
Base case, residual income (RI) valuation model, which we think derives the most accurate
value of the firm given that it takes into account cost of equity. We use a cost of equity of 9.2%
[risk-free rate of 1% (10-year Taiwan government note yield), equity risk premium of 8.7%, and
a beta of 1.0], a medium-term growth rate of 14%, and a terminal growth rate of 3%.

Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected ABF substrate demand from PC and server customers
n Capex cuts; halt to its capacity expansion plan
n Continued yield issues of alternative technology that doesn't require substrate (e.g.,
CoWoP)
Risks to Downside
n Sudden demand shortfall
n Technological change that would not require ABF substrates
n Intensifying competition
n Yield issues or production hiccups when ramping new capacity
Nan Ya PCB (8046.TW)

Base case, residual income (RI) valuation model, which we think derives the most accurate
value of the firm given that it takes into account cost of equity. We use a cost of equity of 9.3%
[risk-free rate of 1% (10-year Taiwan government note yield), equity risk premium of 8.7%, and
a beta of 1.0], a medium-term growth rate of 17%, and a terminal growth rate of 3%.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected ABF and BT demand
n Faster-than-expected pickup in AI and 5G demand

n Stronger-than-expected ASP hike
n Continued yield issues of alternative technology that doesn't require substrate (e.g.
CoWoP)
Risks to Downside
n Sudden demand shortfall, creating headwinds for ABF substrate demand and pricing
n Technological change that would not require ABF substrates
n Intensifying competition

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Document 231: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가현저한 저평가 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가삼성전자(005930) 현저한 저평가현저한 저평가 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

00

삼성전자 (005930/KS) 
현저한 저평가 
3Q26 영업이익 106 조원 예상 
삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익을 106조원 (+18% QoQ)으로 예상한다. 이는 기존 당사 
전망치 111 조원 대비 4.5% 하향된 것이다. 원/달러 환율 가정을 기존 1,450원에서 
1,400원으로 3.4% 하향, MX/NW 사업부의 적자 폭을 확대 반영 (-0.5조원 -> -
1.4조원)했기 때문이다. 주가의 핵심인 메모리 판가는 DRAM +17%, NAND +15%
로 오히려 예상을 소폭 상회할 것으로 예상된다. 출하는 수요 강세에도 불구하고 재고 
부족, HBM 확대에 따른 제한적 공급 여력으로 DRAM +4%, NAND +7%의 기존 예
상을 유지한다. HBM은 분기 최대 출하를 경신하기 시작할 것이다. 또한 AI 강세로 파
운드리 수주 및 가동률의 동반 상승이 지속 중이다. 성과 충당금 반영 등에 따라 단기 
흑자전환 가시성은 제한적이나, DS 사업부 전체적으로 강화되고 있는 국면이다.

2027 년 568 조원 (+55% YoY) 전망 유지. 환율 하락 반영 
SK증권은 삼성전자의 2027년 영업이익 전망치를 568조원으로 유지한다. 원/달러 환
율 가정을 기존 1,440원에서 1,340원으로 7% 하향했음에도, 실적 전망을 유지하는 
이유는 AI 메모리 수요 강세 속 공급 부족 심화, LTA에 따른 Dual market 효과에 의
한 Commodity 메모리 가격 강세, 2027년 HBM 판가 강세 (+103% YoY) 와 출하 
강세, 수익성 회복의 동반, 파운드리 적자 폭 축소의 본격화 전망 때문이다. 2027년 메
모리 출하는 DRAM +22%, NAND +16%, 판가는 DRAM +38%, NAND +28%
를 예상한다. 시장에서 우려하는 메모리 가격 하락, 감익 시나리오는 없을 것이다.

차별적 실적과 주주환원 본격화. 현저한 저평가 
SK증권은 메모리 업황과 삼성전자의 주가가 현저히 저평가 되어있다고 판단한다. 삼성
전자의 2027E P/E는 4.2X에 불과하며, 이익 창출력은 글로벌 최상위권이다. 주주환
원 정책 또한 기존의 FCF 50% 환원이 향후에도 유지될 것으로 가정한다면, 2027년 
193조원, 2028년 222조원으로 현재 시가총액 (우선주 포함) 대비 각각 11%, 13% 
수준에 육박한다. 성장을 위한 투자를 확대하고 있음에도, 대규모의 환원이 가능하다. 
LTA는 AI 시대 메모리 수요의 질과 실적 가시성을 제고시키며, 이는 주주환원의 지속
가능성을 의미한다. 메모리 가격의 높은 기저, LTA 물량의 계약 가격 적용을 감안하면, 
가격 상승률 자체는 안정화될 것이다. 다만 실적 안정성, 가시성, Duration을 확보하기 
시작했다는 점이 훨씬 중요한 가치이며, 아직 시장의 Playbook에 없을 뿐이다. 시대는 
변하고 있고, 시장도 변해야 한다. 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 610,000원을 유지한다. 
2026-10-06

반도체. 한동희 / donghee.han@sks.co.kr / 3773-8826

Dual market화 되는 메모리

자료: SK증권

Document 269: AI・半導体で韓国景気は堅調も、「Ｋ字型」の様相が一段と鮮明に ～家計部門の低迷が李政権の重しとなる可能性～

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI・半導体で韓国景気は堅調も、「Ｋ字型」の様相が一段と鮮明に ～家計部門の低迷が李政権の重しとなる可能性～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-27

Status: ok

### Page 1

Asia Trends

2026 年10 月 27 日

1/5 
AI・半導体で韓国景気は堅調も、「Ｋ字型」の様相が一段と鮮明に 
～家計部門の低迷が李政権の重しとなる可能性～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ 韓国景気は、世界的なＡＩ・半導体関連需要を追い風に、外需主導で堅調な推移が続いてい
る。 ７-９月の実質ＧＤＰ成長率は前期比＋1.0％ （年率換算＋3.93％） 、 前年同期比＋3.4％
となり、2026 年９月までの累計成長率（＋3.6％）は中銀の８月時点の見通し（＋3.3％）を
上回った。メモリ半導体を中心とする財輸出に加え、半導体製造設備やデータセンター関連
の設備投資が成長を支え、2027 年も外需の堅調さが景気をけん引する展開が見込まれる。 
○ 一方、家計部門は力強さを欠いている。インフレ率は中銀目標の２％を上回って推移し、政
府の現金給付などの消費支援も効果が一巡したことから、個人消費は下振れた。成果給の恩
恵は一部の労働者にとどまり、業種間の賃金格差の拡大も懸念される。ソウルを中心に不動
産価格の上昇が続くなか、 家計債務を巡る金融リスクもくすぶっており、 中銀は10 月会合で
政策金利を据え置きつつも、追加利上げに含みを持たせた。KOSPI はピークから約４割下落
しており、金融市場の調整が家計に与える影響にも注意が必要である。 
○ 為替市場では、株価調整とは対照的に、ウォン相場が底堅く推移している。過去最高を更新
した貿易黒字に伴う実需と、中銀のタカ派姿勢が下支えとなっている。 
○ 政治面では、李在明政権の支持率が過去最低に近い水準にある。企業と家計、資産保有層と
それ以外の層との間で景気回復の恩恵に格差が生じる「Ｋ字型」の様相が強まっており、金
融市場の混乱が家計部門に波及すれば、政権の求心力が一段と低下する可能性がある。

【世界的なAI・半導体需要の旺盛さを追い風に、足元の韓国景気は堅調な推移が続いている】 
韓国では、 世界的なＡＩ （人工知能） ・ 半導体関連需要の旺盛な動きが景気の追い風となる展開が
続いている（注１）。韓国は、DRAM やNAND 型フラッシュメモリ、広帯域メモリ（ＨＢＭ）などメモ
リ半導体の大規模量産で世界トップクラスの競争力を有する。旺盛な半導体需要をけん引役に９月
の輸出額は過去最高額を更新するなど、輸出の勢いは強まっている。韓国はＧＤＰに占める輸出の

注１ 10 月１日付レポート「韓国､AI・半導体需要が景気に追い風も､政治への逆風は止まず」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666170.html）

### Page 2

2/5 
比率が４割を上回るなど、 アジア太平洋
地域のなかでも外需依存度が相対的に
高い。こうしたなか、 ７-９月の実質ＧＤ
Ｐ成長率は前期比＋1.0％、年率換算ベ
ースでは＋3.93％となり、 景気の堅調さ
が確認されている （図１）。 中期的な基調
を示す前年同期比ベースの成長率も＋
3.4％と前期（同＋3.7％）から伸びが鈍
化しているものの、 ３四半期連続で３％
を上回る水準で推移している。 
世界的なＡＩ・半導体関連需要の拡大
を反映して財輸出が押し上げられるとともに（図２）、中国人観光客を中心とする来訪者数の増加
はサービス輸出を押し上げるなど、堅調な外需が景気をけん引する動きがみられる。さらに、 ＡＩ ・
半導体関連需要の旺盛さを追い風に、 半導体製造設備やデータセンター関連の投資が拡大するなど、
設備投資も景気を下支えしている。ま
た、政府消費の増加も成長に寄与した。
一方で、政府はイラン情勢の悪化を受け
た原油高の影響軽減を目的に、低所得者
層などを対象に１人当たり 10 万～60 万
ウォンの現金給付を実施したほか、 省エ
ネ家電の購入促進策 （キャッシュバック
の実施）などの消費支援に動いた。しか
し、足元ではその効果が一巡しており、
個人消費は下振れしている。さらに、前
期は在庫調整が進む動きが確認された
ものの、 当期は前期比年率ベースで＋0.32pt と試算されるなど、在庫の積み上がりを示唆する動き
もうかがえる。 
とはいえ、2026 年の９月までの累計ベースの経済成長率は＋3.6％となり、中銀が８月時点で示
した見通し （＋3.3％）を上回っている。 中銀は10 月会合において、2026 年の経済成長率が８月時
点の見通しを大きく上回る可能性を示唆したが、 足元の状況はそうした見方に沿った動きと捉えら
れる。 さらに、2027 年についても、 世界的なＡＩ・半導体関連需要の動向に留意する必要があるも
のの、外需の堅調さが景気をけん引する展開が続く可能性が見込まれる。

### Page 3

3/5 
【家計部門はインフレに直面、賃金上昇の恩恵は一部にとどまり、不動産高騰や株価調整も重し】 
外需は堅調な動きをみせる一方、対照的に個人消費は力強さを欠いている。背景には、2025 年後
半以降のインフレ率が中銀目標（２％）を上回る伸びで推移するなど、実質購買力に下押し圧力が
かかっていることがある。さらに、イラ
ン情勢の悪化を受けた 原油高を背景に
物価上昇圧力が残り、インフレ率は中銀
目標を上回る水準で推移している （図
３） 。これを受けて、政府は燃料価格の上
限規制 を約６ヵ月維持するなど物価対
策に動いている。しかし、足元では中東
情勢 の不透明 感を理由に 原油価格は高
止まりするなど、見通しが立たない状況
が続いている。一方で、半導体産業など
一部企業では、営業利益などの一定割合
（Ｎ％）を従業員の成果報酬の原資とする「Ｎ％成果給」が導入され、他産業にも導入を求める動
きが広がっている。 とはいえ、実際に高額な成果報酬の恩恵を受ける労働者は一部にとどまるなど、
業種間の賃金格差の拡大に繋がる懸念も高まっている。 
韓国ではここ数年、首都ソウルを中心とする不動産価格の高騰が社会問題化してきた。 政府は
2025 年以降に 様々な不動産高騰対策を
打ち出したものの、一連の対策では実需
層の資金調達が困難になる一方、規制対
象外の地域で不動産価格が上昇するな
ど「副作用」も顕在化した。また、政府
は超高額住宅や所有者が居住していな
い住宅に対する課税強化を目指したも
のの、中高所得者層などからの反発を受
けて一部見直しを余儀なくされた。さら
に、一連の政府主導による不動産対策に
対する不満は、６月に実施されたソウル
市長選で与党候補が敗北した一因との見方もある。とはいえ、 足元においてもソウルを中心とする
不動産価格は上昇が続いており （図４） 、住宅関連融資の増加が続くなど、 家計債務を巡る金融リス
クもくすぶっている。中銀は、10 月の定例会合で３会合ぶりに政策金利を据え置いたものの、 追加
利上げに含みを持たせる考えを示した（注２）。背景のひとつには、 不動産市場を巡るリスクがある

注２ 10 月22 日付レポート「韓国中銀、株価急落などで利上げ休止も、金融引き締めの長期化を示唆」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666810.html）

と考えられる。 
2025 年以降の韓国金融市場では、世界的なＡＩ・半導体関連需要の旺盛な動きを追い風に、主要
株価指数（KOSPI）は上昇基調を強めた。
さらに、 政府は2025 年末、 個人投資家が
保有する海外株式を売却して国内株式
に１年間投資した場合、一定の条件の下
で譲渡所得税を最大 100％控除する時限
措置を発表し、2026 年３月に法制化し
た。そのうえ、株価上昇を受けて個人投
資家の間でレバレッジ投資が活発化し
たことも、株価上昇の動きを後押しする
一助となった。しかし、構成銘柄全体の
時価総額に占める割合が５割前後を占
めるサムスン電子とＳＫハイニックスの２社の株価動向にKOSPI が左右されやすい構造にある。 こ
うしたなか、足元ではピークから約４割下落するなど、市場は大幅な調整に直面している（図５）
（注３）。こうした金融市場の不安定な動きも、家計部門に悪影響を与えている可能性がある。

【株価の大幅調整が続いているものの、貿易黒字と中銀のタカ派姿勢がウォン相場を下支え】 
金融市場においては、前述の通り半導体関連株を中心とする株価調整の動きに直面しているもの
の、 通貨ウォン相場は対照的に底堅い動きをみせている （図６） 。 背景には、世界的なＡＩ・半導体
関連需要の旺盛さが輸出を押し上げる
なか、足元の貿易黒字は過去最高を更新
するなど、ウォンに対する実需が生まれ
やすい環境にあることが挙げられる。ま
た、中銀が断続的な利上げを実施すると
ともに、先行きも追加利上げに含みを持
たせるなど「タカ派」姿勢を強めている
ことがある。さらに、 中銀の申総裁は10
月の定例会合後に行った記者会見にお
いて、「足元のウォンの対ドル相場は歴
史的に見れば低水準」 、 「実質実効ベース
でもウォンは依然割安」、「ウォン高は経済的不平等を是正する効果がある」と述べるなど、ウォン
高の経済的な利点を指摘したことも、ウォン相場を後押ししている可能性がある。

経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。
5/5
【金融市場の混乱や家計部門を取り巻く環境が李在明政権の行方を左右する可能性に要注意】
こうしたなか、李在明（イ・ジェミョン）政権の支持率は低水準で推移しており、足元では過去
最低に近い水準で推移している。 このところの支持率低下を巡っては、 若年層などで支持が弱まる
動きがみられるほか、 不動産政策や物価高への不満も影響していると考えられる。 こうした動きは、
政権が支持層をつなぎとめることの難しさを示唆している。足元の景気はいわゆる「Ｋ字型」の様
相を強めるとともに、 企業と家計、 資産保有層とそれ以外の層との間で景気回復の恩恵に格差が生
じており、その格差が一段と拡大する兆しもみられる。このため、先行きは実体経済の堅調さにも

かかわらず、李政権を取り巻く環境が厳しさを増す可能性も考えられる。与党・共に民主党を巡っ
ても、党内では政策運営などを巡る意見対立がみられるなど、火種を抱えている。金融市場の混乱
が家計部門に波及することで、 李政権の求心力をさらに弱める可能性がある点には注意が必要であ
る。

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
We reaffirm our expectation  that the RBA Monetary Policy
Board will again increase the cash rate at the November
meeting. Today’s inflation data and Senate testimony are
the final pieces of information before the RBA Monetary
Policy Board (MPB) meets next week. Recent data and

communication have consistently pointed to the staff
recommending an increase in the cash rate. We expect a
majority of the external members to agree, though some
members might dissent.
Inflation is above target, and above what the RBA’s August
forecasts implied. Some upside risks identified in August have
partially materialised, particularly energy prices. The RBA is
especially worried that above-target inflation will become
embedded in people’s expectations and pricing decisions. It

will therefore deem that further policy tightening is needed.
The RBA has made it clear that it is down-weighting the recent
easing in the labour market, even though this was more than it
forecast. In today’s opening statement  to the Senate hearing,
the Governor said, “Despite the unemployment rate recently
rising a little more than we had expected, the labour market
seems a little tighter than we had previously judged. The
evidence for this is elevated underlying inflationary pressures.

That is, when we exclude some of the large volatile price
movements such as fuel we see inflationary pressure in areas
that reflect domestic demand for goods and services.” In the
Senate, the Governor also said that they were not worried
about the current pace of increase in unemployment.
Of course, this takes no account of the lagged relationship
between the labour market and inflation. The argument is that
if that measure of inflation last quarter was too high, it must

therefore be that the labour market is too tight. Nor does it
allow sufficiently for non-labour cost shocks, which business
surveys indicate are the real issue. But whether this judgement
by the RBA turns out to be right or not will not be revealed
for some time, certainly not soon enough to change its mind
before next week. And while some MPB members might have
a more optimistic view about labour supply trends than the
RBA staff, they are likely to be a minority.

The RBA is also relatively sanguine about the current
downturn in the housing market. The Governor and other
RBA officials are at pains to highlight that they do not target
housing prices; nor should they. The RBA considers that most,
though not all, of the current downswing is an expected effect
of tighter monetary policy.
RBA to hike in November, as inflation remains too high
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
The question then turns to the future beyond November. While

we still expect a hawkish tone in the RBA’s communication
until inflation has clearly turned down, further hikes beyond
next week are not currently our base case. Clearly, though, the
risks of another hike – most likely in February – have increased.
Our current forecast for Q4 inflation would seem to give them
cover for such a move. We note, however, that home-building
costs in the CPI have undershot expectations in the past couple
of months. This might imply a different view of the extent of

domestic demand pressures than the RBA currently believes.
However, a lot can happen in three months, as we have just
seen. The conflict in the Middle East  could escalate further,
lifting energy prices beyond our current base case. It could
also go the other way, though, if the US and Iran manage to
strike a post-midterm-election deal. And as their counterparts
in New Zealand highlighted today, one risk is that the global
economy weakens suddenly. In the post-meeting statement ,

the RBNZ Monetary Policy Committee said, “the world
economy could tip abruptly into a downturn causing weaker
inflation over the medium term.” This risk was something the
RBNZ MPC thought could crystallise in the next six weeks.
Whether on this timetable or something more drawn out, this
possibility does not appear to be on the RBA’s radar. Nor is it
our base case, though we have previously worried that the AI
financing boom could hit a wall quite suddenly.

“We reaffirm our expectation that the RBA
Monetary Policy Board will again increase the
cash rate at the November meeting"

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: the source and persistence of inflation
will remain attentive to any broadening in inflation and related
risks through 2027; although, in the absence of another shock,
we expect them to remain on hold.
The Q3 GDP report certainly supported Chair Warsh’s view

on the breadth of growth, the 3.4% annualised gain for GDP
benefitting from a 3.8% annualised increase in household
consumption, 12.7% growth in non-residential investment
as well as a robust increase in government spending.
Notably though, the contribution from business investment
was effectively offset by net exports’ drag given most of
the componentry related to AI infrastructure investment is
imported. This is not to say that US firms won’t benefit from

the investment once complete, but simply that much of the
initial return goes to the foreign businesses who provide the
necessary capital equipment.
On consumption, the monthly PCE data implies downside risks
remain for the consumer, the increase in nominal spending
seen in the month requiring dissaving and, on a real basis,
consumption flat in the month following a 0.5% gain in
August. Cost-of-living pressures and uncertainty over the
outlook is expected to see household consumption growth

average around trend over the coming year, albeit with
considerable volatility quarter to quarter. This should see PCE
inflation slow back towards its 2.0%yr medium-term target as
the FOMC desires, from 3.6%yr and 3.1%yr for the headline
and core measures respectively as of September.
Across the Atlantic, the ECB also left its key policy
rates unchanged in October. Of note from the meeting
communications, energy prices continue to pose upside risks

for inflation, but also downside risks for growth. "While the
full effects of the energy shock are still unfolding, underlying
inflation has remained broadly stable so far and wages do not
show a material response". "Financing conditions have [also]
tightened", "which is likely to weigh on inflation and growth".
In the press conference, President Lagarde noted that,
between the October and December meetings, a significant
amount of new economic information will be released,

allowing forecasts and risks assessments to be tested.
Based on the October meeting communications, we continue
to expect a rate hike at the December meeting but believe this
will be prove to be the last hike of the cycle. Note though, the
spectrum of risks Europe faces is broad and skewed towards
inflation in the near term, hence the Council’s guidance will
remain hawkish. President Lagarde also announced today that
she will step down as ECB President in February ahead of the

scheduled conclusion of her term next October.
In Australia this week, all eyes were on the September CPI .
Headline inflation rose 0.8% (4.3%yr) in the month, largely
owing to a 9.4% jump in fuel prices and a seasonal surge in
domestic and international holiday travel & accommodation.
The underlying inflation pulse also proved hotter than
anticipated for Q3 overall, up 1.0% (3.7%yr).
While some parts of the basket are seeing disinflation –
housing in particular looks to be cooling faster than expected

as builders lure sales with discounts – other components are
still showing persistence. Highlighting this is market services
ex volatiles, which the RBA watches closely as an indicator
of price pressures related to domestic capacity. In Q3, this
metric rose 1.2% (3.2%yr), and the monthly detail points
to momentum holding up – in line with our expectations for
another chunky read in Q4.
With inflation running well above the RBA’s August forecasts,

and some of the energy risks flagged then materialising, we
continue to expect the Monetary Policy Board will deliver a 25bp
rate hike to 4.85% in November. Beyond November, another rate
hike in February is a risk, but is not currently part of our base case.
Across the Tasman, the RBNZ this week surprised market
expectations for a 25bp rate hike, deciding instead to wait for
further information before taking the next step with policy. The
decision was made 4:3, with the Governor in the ‘on hold’ camp. A

25bp hike in December to 3.00% and further tightening in 2027
(Westpac NZ forecasts a peak of 4.00% in September) seems
probable; however, each decision will depend on fuel prices,
inflation expectations and evidence of second-round pricing
pressures, along with the evolution of global economic risks.
In the US, the FOMC also held rates steady  at its October
meeting. Based on prior comments by key members, the
decision was not a surprise, and neither were Governor Cook

and Cleveland Fed President Hammack’s dissents in favour
of a 25bp hike. The Committee remains positive on economic
activity, characterising it in the statement as “expanding at
a strong pace” (an upgrade from “solid” in September). Chair
Warsh also subsequently emphasised the breadth of economic
growth outside of tech-related investment (evinced by Q3
GDP the day after; see below).
The Committee refrained from spotlighting key price risks in
the statement. However, inflation “remains elevated” and the

FOMC are determined to achieve a “timely” and sustainable
return of annual inflation to 2.0%yr after 6 years above target.
This guidance and the “accumulation of more information will
help the Committee determine the policy stance” suggests
another 25bp hike is likely in December. Thereafter, the FOMC
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

4
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
A lesson to be learned
The RBNZ delivered the biggest shock to markets outside of
a crisis period in over 20 years this week by leaving the OCR
unchanged. Markets had priced around a 98% probability of
a 25bp increase, and all major domestic analysts concurred.

After the CPI data the week earlier, we belatedly shifted our
call to a hike despite plenty of good arguments in favour of
the RBNZ sticking to its plan to pause. With the benefit of
hindsight, too much weight was put on market pricing and
short-term inflation drivers and not enough on the medium
term. That’s an important lesson to be learned.
The highlight of an action-packed week of central banking news
was the unexpected decision of the RBNZ to leave the OCR at

2.75%, despite near unanimous analyst expectations and market
pricing in favour of a 25bp hike. We had shifted sides from a
hold to a hike just a week earlier, in the wake of a CPI outcome
that was weaker than RBNZ expectations on the surface, but
with some evidence of firm core inflation undertones.
While we clearly cannot claim to have gotten this call right,
we always thought that the outcome was not the “done deal”
that markets had priced in. For example, in our preview of the

meeting we noted:
“We still think a more measured tightening approach is
appropriate from here. Raising the OCR at every meeting
until something ‘breaks’ could be a risky strategy if downside
growth concerns crystalise.”
And we saw a vote on the decision as very possible:
“A vote seems very possible as differences in view will likely
remain among the MPC about how much the medium-term
inflation outlook has really shifted in recent weeks.”
The 4:3 vote that was ultimately required to bring the MPC

to a decision is a testament to just how finely balanced the
decision was. We had thought that the hike was perhaps
around a 60-70% probability whereas the Record of Meeting
implies something more like a 50:50 call.
In the final analysis, the RBNZ has merely followed through on
the pause it signalled for this meeting back in the September
Monetary Policy Statement. All MPC members continue to see
a case for a higher OCR in time, but there are different views

around the timing of those increases. Governor Breman noted
that further interest rate increases remain likely in coming
meetings but are conditional on the whether the outlook
evolves in line with expectations. Indeed, the Governor noted
in later comments to press that a December hike was a “high”
probability and that the outlook remains for OCR “hikes” as the
base case. This suggests that the RBNZ’s view on the terminal
OCR hasn’t really shifted down from the 3.25% level presented

back in September. And it may be reasonable to speculate,
subject to data evolving as the RBNZ expects, that a modest
upgrade in those forward OCR forecasts could be in the offing.
So how did we all get this so wrong? This outcome was a real
shock given the historical record shows that over the history of
the OCR the market has without exception correctly predicted
a 25bp hike when the implied probability built into markets
was as high as it was. Clearly some recalibration of the model

is required. We can identify two key areas where lessons
should be learned: too much weight on market pricing; and not
enough on drivers of medium-term inflation.
Market pricing did not adequately reflect the
MPC’s desire to shift strategy .
Firstly, and most importantly, we put too much weight on
market pricing and too little on the RBNZ’s clearly stated
strategy of preferring a pause at the October meeting to
assess the impact of interest rate increases already done. In our

Preview we incorrectly surmised that silence equated to assent
and that market pricing of a hike would make an OCR hike the
less disruptive option – especially given the sense that a hike
to 3% was still likely in December as implied by the RBNZ’s
September forecasts. From a macroeconomic perspective,
raising rates six weeks early is of little consequence.
This logic, as sound as it was, was wrong, as market pricing did
not reflect a large enough change in the medium-term inflation

outlook to justify a change in RBNZ strategy. Markets at least
partially held the view, based on a long history of revealed
RBNZ preference, that the RBNZ keeps moving the OCR in the
same direction until confronted with compelling evidence that
a halt to the cycle is in prospect. That operating approach may
be under review now.
It’s not uncommon for other central banks to take breaks
while amid an interest rate cycle to gather information on
the impact of work already done before moving forward.

This week’s pause by the FOMC in the face of a fierce set of
monthly inflation readings in the last month is an example.
Pausing does not need to imply stopping or reversing. Indeed,
taking a more measured approach could prove to be solid
risk management – as we identified in our MPR preview note.
Risk management concerns probably should have greater
prominence in this cycle given we are seeing a measure of
Kelly Eckhold
Chief Economist NZ

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
supply side cost shocks that might not be persistent. The
Statement of Record contains a wealth of material related to
these sorts of uncertainties and concerns. Markets were not
listening to these concerns, however.
An insufficient focus on drivers of medium-term

inflation
Markets were not listening to the alarm bells because there
was a heavy focus on factors that may be more relevant to the
short-term inflation outlook than medium-term inflation. Top
of mind were the rise in energy prices and fall in the exchange
rate. Both factors look set to significantly boost the short-
term inflation outlook – for example we currently forecast
December quarter annual inflation of 4.3%, with upside risks
from there if fuel prices do not fall over the balance of the

quarter. However, our analysis of the data surprises suggested
no significant shift to the implied RBNZ OCR track one year
ahead. Upward pressure on the future OCR projection from
the lower exchange rate and stronger GDP was balanced
by higher long term interest rates and the lower Q3 CPI
outcome. Higher energy prices were not seen as a key factor
as they don’t impact medium term inflation unless inflation
expectations are dislodged (no evidence was found of that

since September) or if the increase in energy prices is viewed
as persistent (futures markets continue to imply lower forward
energy prices, although MPC hawks clearly see risks of
persistent inflation coming from supply shocks).
The RBNZ’s communications at the MPR and in its aftermath
are directing markets to pay more attention to medium-term
inflation drivers in the future. Hence the references to the
strength of demand and inflation expectations. The risk to

the RBNZ’s inflation forecasts are to the upside, and hence
we might reasonably expect some upgrade of the OCR profile
when we see new forecasts in December. But it’s not a done
deal by any means as data and events between now and then
will dictate what stance the RBNZ takes. This week’s business
and consumer confidence reports do not seem to present many
smoking guns of imminent second-round inflation pressures,
even though business and consumer sentiment seems to be

taking the higher oil price in its stride.
Is core inflation high enough yet to shift the
strategy?
Another interesting point made by the Governor was that the
markets (and analysts) misinterpreted how the September
quarter CPI was read by the RBNZ, which evidently took
greater comfort from the outcome. Governor Breman has noted
she wished the RBNZ has protocols in place to communicate
with markets the RBNZ’s view on that CPI data given that it
seems to have been a factor that figured importantly in at least

some MPC member’s thinking. Headline inflation was lower
than the RBNZ expected and importantly the core inflation
measures, that we had thought might have been of concern to
the MPC, do not yet seem to have been at a level sufficient to
shift the RBNZ from its pause-then-hike strategy. We remain
sceptical that a 3.25% OCR will prove sufficient to bring core
inflation down significantly. But core inflation appears to need
to lift for the MPC to become more agitated. It likely will lift,

but we won’t get more data on that until January 2027.
December hike likely but not a done deal
We expect a further 25bp hike at this year’s final meeting on 9
December and then 25bp increases in the February and March
2027 meetings, followed by a slower pace of increases at the
June and September Monetary Policy Statement meetings.
Our forecast of a peak OCR of 4% from September 2027 has
remained unchanged throughout, as has our assessment of a
3.75% neutral OCR.

That said, in the current uncertain environment, it goes without
saying that the evolution of monetary policy will depend on
how both global events and key data evolve. Therefore, as the
RBNZ noted itself, the exact timing of the tightening profile
is highly uncertain and even the tightening we forecast at the
December meeting should not be regarded as a done deal.
Looking ahead, the ‘hardest’ data to come will be the Q3
labour market surveys where we anticipate a small fall in

the unemployment rate to 5.5% but still moderate wage
pressures. The December quarter RBNZ inflation expectations
surveys will be of significant interest (17, 20 and 24
November) for MPC members looking for persistent inflation
impacts. The usual run of monthly activity and selected prices
indicators will be useful in narrowing down Q4 growth and
inflation momentum. And finally, the MPC’s focus on external
risks will imply keeping an eye on global fixed income, equity

and credit markets for signs of imminent calamity.
Finally, November 7 is the general election. A close race is
envisaged that could impact on the RBNZ’s mandate. We
will be offering client briefings on Monday 9 November and
Tuesday 10 November to outline the immediate implications
of the election outcome for markets and the economy. Contact
your relationship manager for details.

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: RBA Policy Decision (%)
Nov 3, Last: 4.60, Westpac f/c: 4.85
Market f/c: 4.85, Range: 4.60 to 4.85
We reaffirm our expectation that the RBA Monetary Policy
Board (MPB) will decide to raise the cash rate by 25bps to
4.85% at the November meeting.

Inflation is above target, and above what the RBA’s August
forecasts implied. Some upside risks identified in August have
partially materialised, particularly energy prices. The RBA is
especially worried that above-target inflation will become
embedded in people’s expectations and pricing decisions.
Recent data and communication have consistently pointed to
the RBA staff recommending an increase in the cash rate. We
expect a majority of the external board members to agree,

though some members might dissent.
AUS: Oct Cotality Home Value Index (%mth)
Nov 2, Last: –1.1, Westpac f/c: –1.3
The Cotality home value index – covering all of Australia – fell
1.1% in Sep with downward revisions shaving a further 0.4%
off prices. The correction has now seen prices decline 5.2%
from their March peak, annual growth stalling flat. Cotality
reported 97% of capital city suburbs experienced falls over the
three months to September.
The October update is set to be another weak one with an

added hit coming from the RBA’s interest rate rise in late
September. The daily measure (covering the five major capital
cities rather than all of Australia) is tracking a 1.5% decline vs
1.3% in September. The detail points to slightly bigger falls
in Sydney, Brisbane and Perth with Melbourne continuing
to record more moderate declines. Note that seasonality is
usually a slight positive in the October month.
AUS: Sep Dwelling Approvals (%mth)
Nov 2, Last: –6.1, Westpac f/c: –7.0

Market f/c: 2.0, Range: –7.0 to 5.0
Dwelling approvals fell 6.1%mth in August, with the annual
pace easing slightly to 10.3%yr. Weakness centred on unit
approvals with private detached house approvals actually
posting a solid rebound from a July month fall, touching a new
cycle high (up 18.4%yr).
We expect the September update to show a much clearer
weakening. New construction is coming under pressure
from multiple fronts: interest rate rises; a sharp downturn in

established markets; and rising costs – with a great deal of
uncertainty around each of these. New home sales have fell
sharply through the September quarter and we expect to
see detached house approvals show something similar. Unit
approvals are coming off a weak read and should be more
stable. Total approvals are expected to print 7% lower.

### Page 7

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.7
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Sep Goods Trade Balance ($bn)
Nov 5, Last: 0.5, Westpac f/c: 2.0
Market f/c: 1.0, Range: –1.0 to 2.5
Australia’s goods trade surplus narrowed to $0.5bn in August.
Recently, the trade balance has been oscillating between
small deficits and surpluses with significant volatility in the

underlying trade mix.
Swings in non-monetary gold, fuel and cars have all played
a large role, but the most eye-catching result from Aug was
the 79% surge in automatic data processing equipment (ADP)
imports, which captures the investment upswing related to
data centres.
For September, we expect an increase in the surplus as the
ADP import surge unwinds, though we are likely in store for
ongoing monthly volatility in trade for some time.
NZ: Sep Building Consents (%mth)

Nov 3, Last: +5.6%, Westpac f/c: Flat
Annual residential building consent numbers are currently at
a three year high, and we expect that they will remain firm in
the September update. However, monthly consent numbers
are showing signs of peaking, with falls in three of the last four
months. We’re watching to see if that trend has continued,
particularly given the ongoing softness in the housing
market.  In the non-residential space, we’ve seen softness

in the number of retail and office developments, but greater
resilience in the industrial and storage segment.
AUS: Sep Household Spending Indicator (%mth)
Nov 4, Last: flat, Westpac f/c: –0.5
Market f/c: 0.2, Range: –0.5 to 0.8
The household spending indicator was unchanged in August,
annual growth easing to 6.8%yr. The detail showed a modest
rotation away from discretionary categories towards non-
discretionary.
Our Westpac-DataX Card Tracker  showed a decent rise in

the September month although new vehicle sales (which are
not covered by the tracker but are in scope for the spending
indicator) look to have posted a decline. Overall we expect
the indicator to show a 0.5% decline in the September month.
Note that the update with also include Q3 estimates of real
spending – we expect this to show a 0.6%qtr gain with prices
up 1.2%qtr and nominal spend up 1.8%qtr.

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.8
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: Q3 Labour Market Surveys (%)
Nov 4, Unemployment Rate, Last: 5.6, Westpac f/c: 5.5
LCI Private Sector, Last: 0.7, Westpac f/c: 0.5
We expect the unemployment rate to edge down to 5.5%
for the September quarter. Job numbers have been gaining
momentum in recent months, and now look to be enough to

outstrip population growth. However, the existing slack in the
labour market is likely to be keeping wage growth subdued,
despite cost-of-living pressures on workers. Our forecasts are
a little more positive overall than what the RBNZ assumed in
its September MPS forecasts.
US: Oct Employment Report
Nov 6, Payrolls, Last: 29k, Market f/c: 55k, WBC f/c: 75k
Nov 6, U/E rate, Last: 4.2%, Market f/c: 4.2%, WBC f/c: 4.3%
Nonfarm payrolls have shown considerable volatility of late,

July’s -10k followed by a 133k gain in August then a 29k
increase in September. However, the 3-month average of 51k
is likely a good guide on underlying momentum. In October, we
look for a 75k gain, although we expect some of that increase
to be offset by a modest reduction to the prior two months,
keeping the 3-month average around 50k.
While the participation rate has lifted in recent months, it
remains well below the average of 2025. Month-to-month, a

change either way is not guaranteed, but it seems likely that
the participation rate will trend higher in coming months. We
therefore expect the unemployment rate to edge up.

[Table_Tag]    [Table_Summary] 投资要点 
◼ 10月行情的本质：AI信用重定价与内部去拥挤共振： 
◼ 2026年10月万得全A回调12.44%，为2016年1月以来第三大单月跌
幅，呈“上旬下行、中旬弱势、下旬加速”三段式，收于月内低点。与
9月不同，10月美债10年期收益率下行约35.6个基点至4.929%，A股
却加速回调，主导矛盾已从“无风险利率”切换为“AI叙事信用审视+
高拥挤、高杠杆筹码被动去化”。全月日均成交16097亿元、缩量12.1%，
两融余额降1833亿元，属存量资金被动降杠杆，而非恐慌出逃。 
◼ 行业分化：防御与能源相对占优，AI链全面重定价： 
◼ 2026年10月行业分化程度较高，31个申万一级行业中仅5个正收益，
且全部集中于“防御+能源”：食品饮料+5.31%、银行+4.84%、石油石
化+3.34%、煤炭+1.29%、家电+0.56%；红利指数+2.33%。领跌为通信-
30.23%、电子-25.70%、建材-17.96%、综合-17.31%、电力设备-15.88%。
电子1—9月利润同比增1.0倍而股价单月回调25.70%，“业绩向上、
股价向下”表明10月调整可能更多反映估值重估与持仓结构变化，而
非仅由当期盈利表现解释。 
◼ 宏观背景：外部AI信用冲击，内部政策与数据拉锯： 
◼ 2026年10月，海外AI定价从“资本开支扩张=增长”切换为“回报与
信用风险审视”：英特尔单日回调28.58%，亚马逊800亿美元供应商贷
款引发融资结构担忧，存储芯片指数全月回调约24.8%，费城半导体回
调超过15%，纳指回调5.30%，标普500仅回调0.93%。通胀黏性压缩
转松空间：美国9月CPI同比3.7%、PCE同比3.6%，布伦特原油涨
13.6%；10月FOMC维持3.75%—4.00%不变但两名票委主张加息。国
内总量平稳、结构分化，10月制造业PMI回落至49.3%；五中全会后
金融管理部门密集释放“稳市场”信号。 
◼ 市场核心分歧解析： 
◼ 核心分歧集中于六问：10月市场回调更宜理解为“AI信用重定价+内部
筹码去化”的叠加影响；美债收益率回落与A股调整同期出现，说明单
一利率因素对当月走势的解释力有限。政策维稳有助于稳定预期、降低
极端波动风险，后续企稳仍需政策支持、基本面预期与交易结构改善共
同验证，短期或震荡修复，中期仍待信号确认，快速V型反转偏乐观情
形。双创风险度处于历史低位，赔率有所提升；但回调幅度本身并非反
弹持续性的充分条件，胜率提升还需“AI信用冲击缓和+成交承接改善”
双重确认。防御红利空间有限，同时需关注交易拥挤度上升后的性价比
变化。微盘、北证去拥挤在量能层面已较充分，趋势层面仍需跟踪确认。
11月日历效应可作为有限但可参考的季节性线索。 
◼ 11月展望： 
◼ 2026年11月权益综合信号虽转为-2，但万得全A风险度处于2.7%较低
分位，短期下行空间受限、超跌修复概率上升；基准情景由‘震荡磨底’
调整为‘短期震荡修复、中期磨底确认’。历史-2样本偏谨慎，但当前
低风险度与政策维稳密集，短期不宜简单套用历史均值。 
◼ 核心结论与总结 
◼ 2026年10月核心是“全球AI信用重定价”与“内部拥挤端去化”共振
深调，而非国内基本面恶化或海外利率上行；月末政策维稳信号密集，
但指数收于月内低点，政策底信号与市场底筹码仍有时滞。11月是“政
策维稳+超跌约束”与“外部风险窗口+AI信用冲击”的拉锯，基准为短
期震荡修复，中期持续性看‘成交回升+AI链企稳’双确认。结构上短期
超跌成长弹性更大，防御/能源可作底仓；微盘与北证在增量工具落地后
弹性有望改善。 
◼ 风险提示：美国中期选举后地缘升级；美联储12月加息；AI信用冲击
延续；去杠杆负反馈；政策维稳不及预期；数据口径风险。整体需关注
海外利率、地缘冲突、微盘拥挤与政策兑现

### Page 4

4 / 23 
1.   2026年10月行情复盘：一场"不是利率"的调整 
1.1.   传导闭环：为何利率回落未能带动A股同步修复 
 2026年10月A股的回调，拆开看是一条与9月方向相反的四步传导链： 
【叙事端】全球AI信用重定价（英特尔盈利指引不及预期、存储链回调、科技巨
头资本开支与现金流压力） → 【结构端】A股AI硬件链（电子/通信）深度回调→双
创指数失去核心支撑 → 【资金端】高融资余额下的被动降杠杆（两融余额月内大幅回
落、成交缩量）→ 拥挤端（双创/微盘/北证）集中去化 → 【风格端】仅存防御与能源
正收益，市场风险偏好回落，正收益行业范围收窄。 
 这条链的每一环都有数据支撑： 
【叙事端】：2026年10月全球市场对AI的定价逻辑从"资本开支扩张=增长"切换
到"资本开支扩张=回报与信用风险"。标志性事件包括：2026年10月30日英特尔股价
单日回调28.58%（第四季度盈利指引不及市场预期）、亚马逊披露800亿美元供应商贷
款额度引发"AI融资结构"担忧；10月28日SK海力士公布三季度营业利润同比暴增7
倍并启动大规模股东回报后，股价当日上涨10%，但存储链整体在"涨价兑现"与"需求透
支"之间剧烈摆动（存储芯片指数全月回调约24.8%）。"业绩好"与"股价回调"同时出现，
是本轮AI重定价的核心特征——市场开始为"AI资本开支的融资成本与投资回报"定价，
而非为"景气度"定价。 
【结构端】：A股AI硬件链（通信-30.23%、电子-25.70%）的回调幅度远大于其基
本面变化（2026年1-9月电子行业利润同比增长1.0倍，拉动全部规上工业企业利润增
长9.6个百分点），说明10月下的主导变量是估值与筹码，而非业绩。 
【资金端】：全月日均成交16097亿元、环比缩量12.1%；两市融资余额由9月末
25324亿元降至10月29日23492亿元（-1833亿元）——这是2026年以来两融余额幅
度最大的一段回撤，融资盘的被动减持与双创/微盘的回调形成负反馈。 
【风格端】：申万一级行业中仅食品饮料（+5.31%）、银行（+4.84%）、石油石化
（+3.34%）、煤炭（+1.29%）、家用电器（+0.56%）录得正收益，红利指数上涨2.33%、
风险度位于自成立以来66.3%分位；而双创（创业板指-23.02%/科创50-24.13%）、微盘
（-14.74%）、北证50（-14.80%）等拥挤端全面去化。 
根源是"定价锚"的切换：9月A股的下跌是"贴现率上行带来的估值压力"（美债逼
近5.4%），10月美债回落但A股加速下跌，说明当前市场的主导矛盾已从"无风险利率
"切换为"AI叙事的信用与回报审视+内部筹码结构"。隐含的关键推论是：用"美债利率
阈值"（如跌破5.0%）作为A股修复条件在10月被证伪，11月需要观察的是AI信用冲
击的减弱信号（费半企稳、半导体信用利差收敛）与内部杠杆去化的进程（两融余额止
跌）。

### Page 5

5 / 23 
2026年10月深调源于"AI信用重定价+内部去拥挤"的共振，而非国内基本面或海
外利率；这意味着修复路径也不同于前两轮——先等信用冲击减弱与杠杆去化完成，再
谈基本面与政策的重新定价。

1.2.   三段式走势：全月下行，月末收于月内低点 
2026年10月万得全A的下跌呈清晰的"三段式"结构（数据截至2026年10月30
日）：

表1：2026年10月万得全A三段式走势（数据区间2026-09-30至2026-10-30） 
阶段 区间 涨跌 特征 
上旬下行 2026年10月8日 -10月14日 -4.41% 节后调整，AI相关板块开始 走弱 
中旬弱势 2026年 10月 15 日-10月 23日 -2.71% 油价通胀与中选不确定性 压制， 10 月 22 日单日 - 2.45% 
下旬加速 2026年 10月 26 日-10月 30日 -5.85% 10月 27日-2.23%、10月 29 日-2.13%、10 月 30 日- 2.34%，收于月内低点 
数据来源：Wind, 东吴证券研究所 
三段式之中，单日较大回调主要出现在以下四个交易日：2026年 10月 22日（-
2.45%）、10月30日（-2.34%）、10月15日（-2.28%）、10月27日（-2.23%）；反
弹仅两个交易日相对显著：10月23日（+2.02%）与10月28日（+1.55%）。月内最高
收盘6107.00点（10月9日）、最低收盘5425.00点（10月30日），全月收于月内最
低、且当日未见明显的护盘承接。 
这一走势结构提示两个关键信息：其一，10月的回调不是"单日恐慌宣泄"（不同于
9月月末的两根大阴线），而是全月三个阶段连续走弱，外生催化（AI信用、油价、中
选）与内生结构（高融资、高拥挤）持续共振，属于典型的"趋势性去化"而非"情绪性错
杀"；其二，月末创月内新低意味着下跌的动能尚未自然衰竭，11月上旬的修复更可能
来自"外力"（政策维稳、外围风险释放）而非"自发的超跌反抽"。

备注：风险度TR为拥挤度趋势模型因子口径，按各指数自成立以来测算历史分位；常态
区间0-100、极端行情可超越边界。 
三点观察：其一，"越大越抗跌"取代了9月的"越小越抗跌"——上证 50（-2.88%）
与红利（+2.33%）成为仅有的低波品种，而2026年9月表现最强的微盘、双创、北证
50在10月成为去化的主战场，大小盘剪刀差在10月完成了方向反转；其二，风险度已
大面积落入历史低位——万得全A风险度-1.17（2.7%分位）、创业板指风险度-6.06（1.5%
分位）、科创50风险度-7.42（1.8%分位），大盘成长类资产的"超跌程度"超过2024年
初与2022年10月的水平；其三，防御端拥挤度已不低——红利指数风险度74.29（66.3%
分位）、微盘股风险度自9月末的106.1回落至73.9（48.9%分位），防御方向的"低位
安全垫"正在变薄，而拥挤端经过一个月的去化已回到历史中位附近——这为11月的结
构判断提供了重要前提。

2.   行业分化：防御与能源相对占优，AI链全面重定价 
2026年10月行业表现的分化程度明显：31个申万一级行业中仅5个录得正收益，
且全部集中于"防御+能源"；跌幅前三的通信、电子、建筑材料均超过17%，AI硬件链
（通信/电子）与内需周期链（建材/机械/地产）形成了两个主要承压方向。

表3：申万一级行业领涨与抗跌榜（数据区间 2026-09-30至2026-10-30） 
排名 行业 2026年 10月涨跌 归因 
1 食品饮料 +5.31% 高股息+低估值防御属性，油价通 胀链条下的成本传导相对可控 
2 银行 +4.84% 类债属性+三季报息差企稳，农业 银行三季度净利润同比+10.84% 
3 石油石化 +3.34% 布伦特原油单月+13.6%，地缘风 险溢价与炼化景气共振 
4 煤炭 +1.29% 能源比价效应与迎峰度冬预期 
5 家用电器 +0.56% 出口韧性+以旧换新政策延续 
6 交通运输 -0.87% 高股息防御属性，跌幅显著小于 全市场 
7 公用事业 -2.48% 类债属性与电价支撑，相对抗跌 
8 非银金融 -5.22% 成交收缩与融资盘去化压制，但 跌幅仍小于全市场 
数据来源：Wind, 东吴证券研究所

归因上有一个容易被忽略的背离：2026年1-9月电子行业利润同比增长1.0倍（国
家统计局口径，拉动全部规上工业企业利润增长9.6个百分点），而电子板块股价单月
下跌25.70%——"业绩向上、股价向下"的背离，表明10月调整可能更多反映估值重估
与持仓结构变化，而非仅由当期盈利表现解释。这一背离对11月的含义是双面的：一
方面，跌深之后的"业绩确定性"标的具备修复弹性基础；另一方面，只要AI叙事层面的

### Page 8

8 / 23 
信用冲击（资本开支融资、回报审视）未减弱，业绩兑现就难以单独支撑股价——修复
需要"叙事企稳"与"业绩兑现"两者同向。 
三季报的另一面：银行（农业银行单季净利润同比+10.84%、六大行前三季合计净
赚约1.14万亿元）、有色金属（矿企与算力金属企业业绩亮眼）、部分高端制造（源杰
科技前三季归母净利润同比+866.88%、中际旭创+211.81%）的业绩兑现度较高，但股价
表现出现分层——"业绩兑现但筹码拥挤"的品种在去杠杆阶段被优先减持，这是 10月
与以往财报季最大的不同。 
3.   宏观背景：外部"AI信用冲击"，内部"政策与数据的拉锯" 
3.1.   海外：AI叙事重定价、通胀黏性与中选变数 
10月最大的海外变化是市场对AI产业前景的定价逻辑发生变化。 2026年10月
中下旬起，美股对AI的定价从"资本开支扩张→景气上行"转向"资本开支扩张→融资成
本与回报审视"：2026年10月30日英特尔股价单日下跌28.58%（四季度指引不及预
期）、高通下跌11.12%、AMD下跌10.88%；亚马逊因披露800亿美元供应商贷款额度
引发"AI债务融资结构"担忧（此前还出现英特尔、亚马逊信用违约互换报价跳升的报道，
规模级别约123.5个基点）；存储链先涨后跌，存储芯片指数全月下跌约24.8%。这轮
重定价的直接后果是美股涨跌结构剧烈分化：标普500单月仅下跌0.93%，但纳指下跌
5.30%、费城半导体指数下跌逾15%，而标普500等权重指数上涨4.94%——"部分指数
调整相对有限、板块表现分化"是10月全球权益市场的共同特征。 
通胀黏性叠加地缘溢价，压缩了货币政策转松的空间。 2026年10月14日公布的
美国9月CPI同比上涨3.7%（高于预期3.6%、前值3.4%）、核心CPI同比2.6%（高
于预期）；10月30日公布的美国9月PCE同比3.6%（5月以来最高）、核心PCE同
比升至3.1%；同期布伦特原油单月上涨13.6%（收于117.64美元/桶、地缘风险溢价主
导）。油价→通胀→利率预期的链条使"美联储转松预期"进一步后延：10月28日FOMC
（美东时间）维持联邦基金利率目标区间3.75%-4.00%不变，库克与哈马克两名票委主
张加息25个基点，会后声明未对12月是否加息给出明确信号；达拉斯联储主席Logan
（2026年票委）会后表示"仍预计至少需要再加息两次、但可循序渐进"。"暂停但未转
向"是本次会议的核心信息。 
美国中期选举与地缘风险构成11月的最大不确定性组合。 美国中期选举将于2026
年11月3日投票，选前民调与库克政治报告均显示民主党形势改善；据多方报道，美
方已通报海湾盟友准备在选举后（最快11月4日）恢复对伊朗的"短时高强度打击"，卡
塔尔等调解方在10月30日-31日于德黑兰、多哈进行密集斡旋（报道口径）。"选举落
地前的观望+选举后的地缘窗口"是 11月海外风险的两段式结构，对应油价的风险溢价
路径：选举前以降温交易为主（10月下旬油价曾单日回落逾2%）、选举后若打击落地
则溢价再度上冲。

3.2.   国内：数据"总量平稳、结构分化"，政策密集维稳 
三季度经济：总量平稳、投资弱、出口强。 2026年10月19日国家统计局公布三
季度数据：前三季度GDP同比增长4.6%（总量107.11万亿元），三季度单季增长4.5%、
环比回升0.2个百分点；9月规模以上工业增加值同比+5.5%（工业机器人、集成电路产
量增长均超18%）、社会消费品零售总额同比+1.1%（剔除汽车拖累后+3.9%）；但1-9
月城镇固定资产投资同比下降7.2%、地产开发投资同比下降20.6%（新建商品房销售额
同比-13.9%），投资端是总量数据中最弱的一环。物价方面：9月CPI同比+0.9%（核心
CPI+1.0%）、PPI同比+5.0%（2022年6月以来最高，其中汽油价格同比+16.6%）——
PPI高读数由能源与输入性因素主导，"再通胀的交易属性"强于"需求属性"。9月城镇调
查失业率5.2%、较上月回落0.1个百分点。 
金融与利润：直接融资占比抬升、工业利润保持两位数增长。 2026年10月14日
央行公布前三季度金融数据：社会融资规模增量累计27.46万亿元（同比少 2.63万亿
元）、9月末社融存量同比+7.1%、M2同比+7.2%、前三季度人民币贷款增加11.52万亿
元；结构上债券与股票融资在社融增量中占比约49.5%、超过贷款占比约7个百分点。

2026年 10月 27日公布的工业利润数据显示：1-9月规模以上工业企业利润同比增长
15.5%（利润总额60337.4亿元）、9月单月同比+14.4%（今年6月以来最高），其中电
子行业利润同比增长1.0倍、高技术制造业利润增长52.0%。"金融数据总量收敛、结构
向直接融资倾斜"与"工业利润由AI链与能源链拉动"并存，是10月基本面数据的主线。 
政策端：从"金融街论坛"到"五中全会"的密集维稳信号。 政策线索按时间排序：
①2026年10月19日-21日金融街论坛年会召开，央行行长潘功胜表示将推动退出存贷
款基准利率、完善"DR001+贷款市场报价利率"的双定价基准体系；②10月26日-29日
党的二十届五中全会在北京召开（研究持之以恒推进全面从严治党若干重大问题、审议
通过《决定》，10月30日举行新闻发布会解读）；③10月30日央行、证监会、金融
监管总局等金融管理部门分别召开会议传达全会精神，其中证监会明确"全力稳市场稳
预期，深化投融资综合改革，完善全链条监管体系，持续提升资本市场韧性和信心"；
④10月30日央行公告公开市场操作"换锚"：自11月2日起每个工作日开展隔夜逆回
购操作招标（操作利率1.25%），7天期逆回购调整为固定数量、利率招标、多重价位
中标并主要在税期、月末等时点开展；⑤全国人大常委会第二十五次会议将于11月2
日-6日举行，金融法草案将提请二次审议（拟加强对资产管理产品与服务的统一规范、
跨境金融活动监管）。"稳市场"表述与操作层面的"换锚"是10月末政策端最重要的边际
变化，其含义是短端利率调控框架向价格型进一步转型、并为跨月流动性提供新的工具
安排。 
10月31日公布的中国10月PMI回落至收缩区间： 制造业PMI 49.3%（前值50.1%）、
非制造业商务活动指数49.7%、综合PMI产出指数与两项分项同向回落——PMI重回荣
枯线下方，与9月数据的"温和改善"形成反差，也为11月政策加码提供了依据。

4.   市场核心分歧解析 
分歧一：这轮下跌的主因是"海外利率"还是"AI叙事+内部结构"？ 市场在 10月
仍习惯用"美债利率"解释A股，但数据不支持：10月美债10年期收益率下行了约35.6
个基点至4.929%，A股却加速下跌。我们的回答是后者：下跌的主因是"AI叙事的信用
重定价"与"高拥挤、高杠杆筹码的被动去化"，海外利率10月反而是"逆风减弱"的。这
一判断的可检验推论是：若11月A股企稳，应首先看到费半企稳、两融余额止跌与AI
链缩量收敛， 而非美债进一步下行——用利率阈值跟踪A股修复条件在10月已被证伪。 
分歧二：政策维稳信号能否让市场"见底"？ 10月末维稳信号密度为2026年最高
（五中全会+证监会“全力稳市场稳预期”+央行换锚+金融法二审），政策信号主要作用
于预期与流动性，而“政策底”向“市场底”的过渡通常还需要筹码出清（融资盘、拥挤端）
与外部风险释放（中选、地缘）形成共振。10月30日指数收于月内低点，量价特征显
示筹码出清仍在推进，完成度需后续数据进一步验证。基准判断是：政策维稳有助于降
低尾部风险、稳定预期与流动性环境；趋势扭转则需要更多条件配合。见底形态更可能
经历缩量整固和反复确认，V型反转可作为乐观情形之一。 
分歧三：超跌成长（双创）能否凭借"跌得多"反弹？ 创业板指风险度分位已降至
1.5%（自成立以来）、科创50风险度分位降至1.8%，“超跌”程度接近或超过历史较低
分位，赔率有所提升。但10月的一个重要证据是：跌幅本身不构成反弹持续性的充分

### Page 12

12 / 23 
条件——本轮回调的传导源（AI信用冲击）仍在全球层面消化（英特尔、亚马逊等事
件），筹码层面（融资盘、主题基金、部分量化产品）的出清进度也需要从价格之外的
多维度确认。10月下旬以来双创曾出现多次单日反抽（10月23日、10月28日），显
示一定弹性，但连续性仍需成交与信用信号配合。判断：双创具备“赔率”，而“胜率”的
确认需要两个条件——AI信用冲击减弱（费半止跌+相关信用指标收敛）与成交结构改
善（缩量止跌或放量承接）；两者出现前，反弹持续性宜按逐步验证处理，关注技术性
反抽向趋势修复转化的条件。 
分歧四：防御与红利还能不能追？ 10月红利指数上涨2.33%、银行上涨4.84%，
防御与能源是当月主要的正收益方向；但防御的拥挤度已经抬升（红利指数风险度74.29、
处自成立以来66.3%分位），且银行业具有"高位放量滞涨"的微观特征（工商银行月内
创历史新高后于月末走弱）。我们的回答：防御依然是基准情景下的相对占优方向，但
其"相对收益属性"强于"绝对收益空间"——在油价与利率的再通胀组合下，防御的性价
比依赖"风险偏好继续收缩"这一前提，该前提若被政策维稳与中选落地削弱，防御的进
攻性会快速衰减。 
分歧五：微盘/北证/双创的"去拥挤"结束了吗？ 10月微盘股风险度由 106.1回落
至73.9（自历史98.2%分位回落至48.9%分位）、短期动量降至9.1，北证50月内回调
14.80%、收于月内低点。拥挤度指标已明显回落，调整进程进入“后半程”，但结束与否
仍需观察；两市融资余额10月下降1833亿元，月末企稳信号仍需跟踪。判断：去拥挤
已进入后半程，短期继续大幅去化的空间有限；若全市场风险偏好修复与政策工具落地，
微盘/北证/双创有望出现阶段性反弹，中期弹性看增量资金。 
分歧六：11月的"日历效应"这次能否相信？ 历史上11月是A股偏强的月份（2000
年以来26次中18次上涨、均值+1.83%），"大幅回调后的次月"的统计（56.1%胜率）
同样偏正面；但2026年以来日历效应已多次失效（3月、7月、9月均未遵循季节性）。
我们的回答：日历效应只提供"弱概率支撑"，不构成方向判断；11月的方向由"政策维稳
的落地强度"与"AI信用冲击的减弱速度"共同决定。

5.   2026年11月展望 
5.1.   量化模型：权益综合信号转为-2 
"权益综合信号"月频量化择时模型以基本面、资金面、国际面、估值面、技术面五
维度打分合成综合信号（正看多、负看空、0中性），据以判断万得全A月度方向，样
本自2016年1月起、数据截至2026年10月31日。

数据来源：Wind, 东吴证券研究所 
信号解读：2026年11月综合信号-2，其中估值面（-1）与国际面（0）由9月末评
级下调所致，技术面（-1）反映趋势与动量仍待修复，资金面（+1）是唯一正值（政策
维稳与流动性工具"换锚"）。历史上-2信号14次样本中，正收益概率28.57%、平均收
益-2.09%——在所列历史样本中，-2信号对应的平均收益低于-1信号（-1的平均收益为
-1.06%），需要以谨慎为基调；但需要注意，当前万得全A风险度（-1.17、2.7%分位）
所对应的"低位约束"也是历史少见，模型信号与结构数据的组合指向"下行空间受限、但
趋势反转未至"——即：负收益的概率高、但"负收益的幅度"未必重复历史均值，短期更
应理解为“下行空间受限、超跌修复概率上升”；中期趋势反转仍需成交与AI信用信
号确认。负收益概率虽高，但短期修复窗口正在打开。

### Page 14

14 / 23 
5.2.   宽基方向：五维框架 
五个维度并非平行，其中 F2（政策+海外）主导方向，F1 看下行空间，F3 看上行
斜率，F4、F5 分别提供概率参考与最终情景加权。落到2026年11月： 
F1 风险收益结构：整体低位、防御端已不再便宜 
图1：万得全 A指数风险趋势模型（2026/10/30）

数据来源：Wind，东吴证券研究所

2026年10月30日，万得全A风险度-1.17（自成立以来2.7%分位），为2000年
以来历史第11低位区域、低于2016年以来任何一次月末水平（2024年9月20日低点
为-0.55、2022年10月为-0.46）。历史上万得全A风险度≤0的交易日共223天，近五
次典型出现时点及此后表现：2024年9月20日（未来1个月+28.4%、3个月+32.2%）、
2023年6月12日（+0.4%、-1.2%）、2022年10月17日（+0.7%、+3.1%）、2018年
7月9日（-5.1%、-6.9%）、2016年2月15日（+1.6%、+3.5%）。"低风险度"是赔率信
号而非胜率信号：五次中1个月后4次为正但分化较大（+28.4%至-5.1%），3个月后3
次为正——低位意味着"向下的空间有限、向上的路径依赖催化"，与正文分歧二的"政策
底≠市场底"相互印证。 
结构上分两端：超跌端（创业板指-6.06、科创50-7.42、中证500-3.81，分位均处自
成立以来1%-2%较低区间）具备厚安全垫；防御端（红利指数74.29、66.3%分位；银行

### Page 15

15 / 23 
股月内创新高）拥挤度已抬升，"便宜"的成分下降。 
结论：整体风险度较低构成11月"下行空间受限"的核心依据，但方向性机会需要
超跌端出现"催化触发"，防御端的相对优势依赖风险偏好继续收缩。 
F2 主导逻辑边际变化：政策维稳"密度上升" vs 外部风险"两段式" 
强化项（国内）：五中全会（10月26日-29日）后金融管理部门密集表态，证监会
"全力稳市场稳预期"；央行公开市场"换锚"（11月2日起隔夜逆回购常态化、7天期逆
回购调整为利率招标）改善短端利率调控的灵活性；全国人大常委会（11月2日-6日）
审议金融法草案二审稿；政策工具箱在"稳市场"方向的可用性上升。 
加重项（海外，选举前）：美国中期选举前的观望效应（资金降低风险承担），美
股AI链的信用冲击（英特尔、亚马逊事件）仍待减弱，费城半导体指数10月下跌逾15%
后尚未出现趋势性企稳。 
加重项（海外，选举后）：美方被报道筹备选举后恢复对伊朗打击（最快11月 4
日）、卡塔尔斡旋结果待定；12月FOMC（12月8日-9日）加息悬念未解（10月FOMC
两名票委主张加息、Logan称至少还需两次加息）。 
缓和项：美债10年期收益率已回落至4.929%（"利率逆风"边际减弱）；油价在斡
旋进展下出现过单日逾2%的回落（若选举后局势降温，或触发通胀-利率预期的连锁缓
和）；美元在月内冲高后回落。 
结论：11月短期更可能呈现政策维稳与超跌修复主导的震荡上行/修复格局，中期
取决于中选后地缘路径。外部风险两段式意味着短期修复可期、中期波动仍在。11月第
一周的美国中期选举与随后的地缘窗口是本轮宏观定价的"分水岭事件"。 
F3 资金博弈结构：缩量去杠杆的后半程 
2026年10月全市场日均成交16097亿元、较9月的18315亿元缩量12.1%，月末
五个交易日成交在14264亿-19729亿元区间波动、无持续放量迹象；两市融资余额由9
月末的25324亿元降至10月29日的23492亿元（-1833亿元），降幅为2026年以来最
大，且月末仍未出现明确的止跌形态。另一方面，流动性总量维持宽松：央行10月30
日当周公开市场净投放14110亿元（创三个半月单周新高），叠加11月2日起隔夜逆
回购常态化操作的"换锚"，资金利率的波动率预计降低。 
结论：11月资金面处于杠杆去化后半程，短期增量资金系统性入场条件尚不充分，
但政策工具与流动性‘换锚’有望带来边际回流；行情短期以结构修复为主，中期若两融
止跌、成交回升，修复范围有望扩散。 
F4 日历效应与均值回归 
基于万得全A指数（881001.WI）日频原始数据（2000-01至2026-10，共321个月）

实测： 
表10：大幅回调月后次月日历效应统计（样本区间 2000-01至2026-10） 
统计 样本 结果 
单月回调≤-5%后次月 67次（含 2026年 10 月），可统计66次 上涨 37次、胜率 56.1%；次月均值 +0.30%、中位+1.08% 
其中：2020年以来 13次（含 2026年 10 月），可统计12次 上涨 8次、胜率 66.7%；次月均值 +1.12% 
历次 11月（季节性） 26次 上涨18次、胜率69%；均值+1.83%、 中位+2.44% 
数据来源：Wind, 东吴证券研究所 
结论：2026年10月万得全A回调12.44%，达到模型设定的大幅回调阈值；历史
规律显示"大幅回调后的次月偏反弹"（全样本胜率56.1%、2020年以来66.7%），11
月本身也是历史偏强月份（69%胜率）。但需警惕两点：其一，"大幅回调后的次月"的
均值仅+0.30%（中位数+1.08%），且样本中不乏继续回调的案例（2022年3月-7.5%
后次月-9.5%、2022年9月-7.6%后次月-2.5%）；其二，2026年以来日历效应已多次失
效（2026年3月-8.7%后次月+8.7%兑现反弹， 但2026年7月-13.1%后的8月仅+5.4%、
9月-5.3%后的10月更是-12.4%）。综合看，日历效应对11月构成"弱概率支撑"，可
作为"下行空间受限"的辅助证据。

5.4.   行业观察（产业链基本面五条线索） 
线索一：能源与通胀链（石油石化+煤炭+农业）——布伦特原油10月上涨13.6%
（地缘溢价主导），石油石化板块+3.34%、煤炭+1.29%为仅有的两个正收益周期方向。
11月的核心变量是"中选后地缘路径"：若打击落地，油价与炼化景气的交易延续；若斡
旋达成降温，需防溢价回吐。该线索是当前"基本面与事件驱动共振度"最高的方向。 
线索二：防御/红利链（银行+食品饮料+公用事业+交通运输）——类债属性在"缩量
+去杠杆"环境中占优，但拥挤度抬升（红利指数66.3%分位）与"高位滞涨"微观特征提
示：防御的方向价值仍在、上行性价比下降。三季报验证：银行净利润增速企稳（六大
行前三季合计净赚约1.14万亿元）、食品饮料龙头业绩平稳。 
线索三：超跌成长链（电子+通信+计算机）——电子行业三季报净利润同比增长1.0
倍、通信设备（光模块链）业绩兑现度高（中际旭创前三季归母净利润同比+211.81%），
"业绩与股价背离"意味着一旦AI叙事企稳，该方向可能具备较大的修复弹性；11月的
观察点是费城半导体指数的企稳形态与存储链价格预期（涨价逻辑是否重启）。

### Page 18

18 / 23 
线索四：政策托底链（地产+基建+消费）——前三季度地产投资-20.6%显示基本面
压力仍在；政策端（房贷贴息、现房销售改革扩围至7城）以"精准托底"为主，总量弹
性有限。人大常委会（11月2日-6日）金融法二审与"十五五"开局项目落地节奏是政策
链的观察窗口。该线索的参与价值集中于"政策表态期"的博弈性机会，而非趋势性机会。 
线索五：北交所链（北证50+专精特新）——北交所354家公司三季报披露收官、
37家净利翻倍；北证50月内-14.80%后估值回落（中证指数公司口径市盈率33.66倍），
但增量资金（北证50ETF进度）落地前，修复更可能滞后于主板。该线索属"赔率型"方
向：较低的成交占比与风险度意味着若增量工具落地并带来实际资金流入，相关板块的
修复弹性值得关注。

6.   核心结论与总结 
6.1.   复盘结论

### Page 19

19 / 23 
2026年10月A股的核心特征是"全球AI信用重定价"与"内部拥挤端去化"的共振
深调：万得全A单月-12.44%（2016年1月以来第三大单月跌幅），创业板指-23.02%、
科创50-24.13%（均为历史第二大单月跌幅），通信、电子领跌（-30.23%、-25.70%），
仅防御与能源录得正收益（食品饮料+5.31%、银行+4.84%、石油石化+3.34%）。与9月
加息相关预期带来的估值压力不同，10月美债收益率回落了约35.6个基点、A股却加
速下跌——主导矛盾已从"无风险利率"切换为"AI叙事的信用审视+内部筹码结构"：全
月缩量12.1%（日均成交16097亿元）、两市融资余额下降1833亿元，量价形态属"存
量资金被动降杠杆"而非"恐慌式出逃"。月末（10月26日-29日）五中全会召开、金融
管理部门密集释放"稳市场"信号，指数却收于月内低点——政策底的"信号"与市场底的
"筹码"之间仍有时滞。 
6.2.   展望结论 
2026年11月是"政策维稳+超跌约束"与"外部风险窗口+AI信用冲击"的拉锯，基准
判断为短期震荡修复，中期磨底确认：下有风险度低位（万得全A 2.7%分位）与政策底
封住深度回调空间，上有中选后地缘窗口、12月FOMC悬念与去杠杆未完成约束反弹
斜率。与10月初"顶下移"的修正不同，11月展望的核心修正是"底渐近"：修复的启动需
要"成交回升+AI链企稳"的双确认（详见5.5量化跟踪指标），而模型11月信号-2（历
史上-2样本平均收益-2.09%）提示趋势层面的谨慎仍未解除。结构上，基准情景的占优
方向仍为防御（银行/食品饮料/公用事业）与能源（石油石化/煤炭），超跌成长（电子
/通信）具备赔率、等催化；微盘与北证50属"去化后半程"，反弹弹性弱于主板。 
6.3.   核心预期差清单 
表14：核心预期差清单（2026年11月） 
市场共识 我们的不同判断 
"美债回落将带动A股修复" 
10月数据显示：美债下行35.6bp、A股延续回调；修复条件应换锚为"AI信用冲击
减弱+杠杆去化完成"，美债阈值参考权重下调 
"五中全会维稳信号意味着
政策强刺激" 
全会主题为从严治党与《决定》，金融管理部门的"稳市场"表述属预期管理与制度
性建设，增量刺激需等人大常委会（11月2日-6日）与后续政策窗口确认 
"跌深了就该反弹" 
双创风险度为历史较低分位（1.5%-1.8%分位）提供赔率；胜率确认需要费半企稳+
成交承接。 
"防御/红利已拥挤，行情将
切向成长" 
防御拥挤度抬升属实（66.3%分位），但在"缩量+去杠杆+地缘不确定"的组合下，防
御的相对优势依赖风险偏好收缩；切换的确认信号是风险度回升与成交放量。 
"油价见顶、通胀交易结束" 
油价的地缘溢价由中选后路径决定：打击落地则溢价上冲、斡旋达成或将回吐；通
胀链的定价是"事件驱动"而非"趋势驱动"，需结合11月4日的时间窗口审慎跟踪 
"北证50与微盘已跌透" 
风险度回落至中位但动量较低、月内收于低点；去拥挤的"量"完成大半。反弹的触
发依赖增量资金工具（北证50ETF）与全市场风险偏好修复

6.4.   关键跟踪变量

### Page 20

20 / 23 
2026年11月判断的证伪与验证，取决于以下几个可跟踪信号：① 美国中期选举
（11月3日）及选举后的地缘窗口（11月4日前后）——油价与全球风险偏好的分水
岭；② AI信用冲击的减弱信号——费城半导体指数企稳形态、科技巨头信用利差与资
本开支指引变化；③ 两融余额与成交额——去杠杆进程（止跌）与增量资金（1.8万亿
元）的双观察；④ 全国人大常委会（11月2日-6日）——金融法二审与"十五五"开局
政策信号；⑤ 央行"换锚"（11月2日起）后的短端利率与流动性分布——隔夜逆回购
常态化对资金面的实际影响；⑥ 美联储12月会议（12月8日-9日）预期路径——CME
加息概率与美债反应；⑦ 万得全A风险度与微盘短期动量——低位修复与去拥挤结束
的量化确认。 
6.5.   上期观点回顾与验证 
上期《A股9月复盘与10月展望--缩量调整中的结构性分化，与政策托底的十字路
口》对2026年10月作出多项前瞻判断，现以2026年10月实际数据完整回验。 
判断一（10月行情情景）：基准"震荡修复、非单边"，明确否定"延续下跌"。 2026
年10月万得全A实际下跌12.44%（三段式：上旬-4.41%、中旬-2.71%、下旬-5.85%），
为2016年1月以来第三大单月跌幅。判定：错。 偏离归因：低估了"全球AI信用重定
价"的传导强度与高融资余额下"拥挤端集中去化"的负反馈；当时对"缩量制约上行"的判
断方向正确，但把下行风险主要挂在"海外高利率"（实际美债回落）与"12月日历效应"
上，错置了风险来源。可复用的教训：当估值、筹码与叙事三者同时存在脆弱性时，应
按"最坏情景路径"而非"基准路径"准备。 
判断二（结构判断）："微盘股相对承压、超跌成长相对占优"。 2026年10月：万
得微盘股指数-14.74%（跑输万得全A约2.3个百分点）——承压兑现； 创业板指-23.02%、
科创50-24.13%（分别跑输万得全A约10.6、11.7个百分点）——"超跌成长占优"被完
全反向验证。判定：部分对（微盘承压正确、超跌成长占优错误）。 教训："超跌"不是
发动条件，"超跌+催化+筹码出清"才是；在外部信用冲击未减弱时，跌幅越深的方向反
而可能因筹码结构（融资盘、主题基金集中）而继续跑输。 
判断三（行业方向）："政策托底链、超跌修复链占优、资源品承压"。 2026年10
月：政策托底链中银行+4.84%、食品饮料+5.31%（边际上由"托底链"实际演绎为"防御链
"，方向正确）；超跌修复链（电子-25.70%、电力设备-15.88%）方向错误；资源品整体
并未承压——石油石化+3.34%、煤炭+1.29%（能源链走强），仅有色金属-14.62%（工
业金属承压），内部严重分化。判定：部分对（防御方向正确、超跌修复与"资源品承压
"总体错误）。 教训：资源品的定价要看"能源（地缘溢价）"与"工业金属（全球需求）
"的拆分，不能作为整体判断。 
判断四（量化模型）：2026年10月综合信号-1（模型方向判断偏弱）。 万得全A 
10月下跌12.44%，方向兑现、且为-1信号历史上幅度最大的兑现之一。判定：对。 且
本次验证了"The model > 结构直觉"的规律：当模型信号与"政策底、风险度低位"等结构

### Page 21

21 / 23 
信号冲突时，模型侧再次胜出（连续第4个月方向正确）。 
判断五（微盘择时）：2026年10月9日首次触发"局部顶左侧信号"（短期动量由
62.94降至35.69），提示微盘拥挤风险。 此后微盘股于10月15日-16日转向"局部顶
右侧信号"、10月19日起进入"局部顶，右侧信号2"持续状态，全月下跌14.74%、风险
度由106.1回落至73.9。判定：对，且信号链条完整兑现（左侧→右侧→回撤）。 可复
用经验："高拥挤+动量背离（风险度新高而短期动量骤降）"的顶部信号，在外部冲击来
临时兑现速度较快。 
判断六（情景框架）：基准"震荡承压、6000-6400区间"、悲观"下行至5800附近"。 
实际走势跌破基准区间下沿并突破悲观情景的指示位（收于5425.00点）。判定：基准
错、悲观方向对但幅度被突破。 教训：当情景框架的"基准"依赖某一条件（此处为"地
缘不实质升级"）而该条件在月内即被打破（霍尔木兹油轮事件、美方筹备选举后打击）
时，应即时切换至悲观路径跟踪，而非等待月末复盘。 
判断七（风格推演）：红利/价值"由占优转为震荡"、大盘成长"超跌待修复"、微盘
"谨慎"、资源"震荡"。 实际：红利+2.33%、银行+4.84%（防御持续占优，"转震荡"判断
偏保守）；大盘成长方向判断落空、继续回调（同期创业板指-23.02%）；微盘"谨慎"正
确；能源强于"震荡"预期。判定：部分对（微盘、能源判断正确；防御与成长方向偏差）。 
判断八（跟踪阈值）："美债10年期跌破5.0%则压制缓和、成长修复弹性打开"。 
条件实际触发（10月下旬美债下行至4.929%），但A股并未修复、成长加速下跌。判
定：阈值机制失效。 这是本期最重要的方法论修正：A股修复条件的跟踪锚应从"美债
利率"切换为"AI信用冲击减弱（费半/信用利差）+内部杠杆去化（两融/成交）"，美债
与美元降级为背景变量。 
判断九（风险提示）：微盘拥挤度与局部顶风险、地缘冲突升级风险、政策效果不
及预期风险。 三项风险中，微盘与地缘两项均兑现（微盘-14.74%；霍尔木兹油轮事件、
美方筹备选举后打击），政策链风险亦兑现（地产-15.01%）；唯"美联储加息风险"在10
月未兑现（FOMC按兵不动、但12月悬念未解）。判定：风险清单命中率高，但"风险
清单命中"未能转化为"方向判断正确"——风险提示与情景框架之间需要更刚性的联动
机制（本期已在本报告F5情景表中强化，见"触发条件"列）。

7.   风险提示 
1) 美国中期选举后地缘升级风险（概率中、影响高） 
美方被报道筹备在选举后恢复对伊朗的打击（最快11月4日），若落地，原油与
能源价格上行将强化全球再通胀、推高利率中枢并压制风险偏好。跟踪预警指标为霍尔
木兹海峡通航状态、布伦特原油价格（上破125美元则升级确认）与卡塔尔斡旋进展。

### Page 22

22 / 23 
2) 美联储12月加息风险（概率中、影响高） 
10月FOMC按兵不动但两名票委主张加息、12月悬念未解； 若加息超出市场预期，
美债收益率与美元可能面临上行压力。跟踪预警指标为CME加息概率（升破50%则偏
空）与美债10年期收益率（上破5.2%则压制加剧）。 
3) AI信用冲击延续风险（概率中、影响高） 
英特尔、亚马逊等事件显示AI叙事正从"景气定价"转向"信用与回报审视"，若再出
现巨头评级下调、融资受阻或资本开支指引大幅收缩，全球科技股与A股AI链将二次
承压。跟踪预警指标为费城半导体指数、科技巨头信用违约互换报价与资本开支指引。 
4) 去杠杆负反馈风险（概率中、影响中） 
两市融资余额10月下降1833亿元且月末未止跌；若11月上旬出现加速，微盘、
双创等拥挤端可能再度脉冲式下跌。跟踪预警指标为两融余额日度变化、微盘股短期动
量与成交结构。 
5) 政策维稳不及预期风险（概率中、影响中） 
五中全会后金融管理部门的"稳市场"表述若缺乏后续增量工具（流动性、资本市场
制度），政策底的预期支撑将衰减。跟踪预警指标为人大常委会审议内容与证监会后续
举措、成交额变化。 
6) 数据口径风险（概率低、影响低） 
行业/风格/指数涨跌为原始数据2026年9月30日至2026年10月30日实算；风险
度为因子数据 TR（各指数自成立以来分位）；美债收益率为美国财政部日度收盘口径
（2026年10月30日：10年期4.929%）；全球资产表现部分采用市场收盘与媒体转述
口径（已在表5标注）。海外市场与事件信息以公开报道为准，单一来源表述已标注。

东吴证券投资评级标准 
投资评级基于分析师对报告发布日后 6至 12个月内行业或公司回报潜力相对基准表现
的预期（A 股市场基准为沪深 300 指数，香港市场基准为恒生指数，美国市场基准为标普 
500 指数，新三板基准指数为三板成指（针对协议转让标的）或三板做市指数（针对做市转
让标的），北交所基准指数为北证 50指数），具体如下： 
公司投资评级： 
买入：预期未来 6个月个股涨跌幅相对基准在 15%以上； 
增持：预期未来 6个月个股涨跌幅相对基准介于 5%与 15%之间； 
中性：预期未来 6个月个股涨跌幅相对基准介于-5%与 5%之间； 
减持：预期未来 6个月个股涨跌幅相对基准介于-15%与-5%之间； 
卖出：预期未来 6个月个股涨跌幅相对基准在-15%以下。 
行业投资评级： 
增持： 预期未来 6个月内，行业指数相对强于基准 5%以上； 
中性： 预期未来 6个月内，行业指数相对基准-5%与 5%； 
减持： 预期未来 6个月内，行业指数相对弱于基准 5%以上。 
我们在此提醒您，不同证券研究机构采用不同的评级术语及评级标准。我们采用的是相
对评级体系，表示投资的相对比重建议。投资者买入或者卖出证券的决定应当充分考虑自身
特定状况，如具体投资目的、财务状况以及特定需求等，并完整理解和使用本报告内容，不
应视本报告为做出投资决策的唯一因素。

### Page 1

European bank M&A has seen a resurgence in recent years, and
2027 should stay busy. Domestic deals still dominate, while
cross-border consolidation is harder but is also picking up.
Regulation is becoming more supportive, but politics and
execution still matter. Expect more deals, but a transformation
of Europe's banking sector is still some way oﬀ
Jesse Norcross
UniCredit and Commerzbank are at the centre of Europe’s closely watched
push towards cross-border banking consolidation

European bank M&A is back
European bank M&A has seen a strong resurgence, with several
large-scale deals making headlines. UniCredit’s high-stakes
takeover of Commerzbank, once deemed very unlikely but now
moving closer to completion, could be Europe’s most signiﬁcant
cross-border transaction in years. Italy remains one of Europe’s
bank M&A hotbeds, with multiple banks taking part in the ongoing
consolidation wave. Other banks have also been active, and we

expect M&A to remain elevated in 2027.

Year-to-date M&A deal value has already surpassed the 2025 total,
reaching €53bn including the UniCredit-Commerzbank transaction
and based on deals valued at €100m or more. Furthermore, deals
pending or intended total €39bn, which includes the potential
takeover of Banca Monte dei Paschi by Intesa Sanpaolo, currently
valued at around €34bn. All in, this would bring deal value back to
levels not seen since the early 2010s.

The rationale for European bank M&A has changed. After the
ﬁnancial crisis, deals were mostly about repair, followed by a wave
of deals involving stronger domestic banks buying weaker rivals.
Today, the backdrop has shifted – proﬁtability and capital levels are
stronger, making the obvious cost-eﬃciency story less compelling

on its own. Instead, banks are using M&A to add scale, deposits and
fee-generating diversiﬁcation, while the need to close the gap with
US rivals remains a key driver.
The industrial logic has also strengthened, as ﬁxed costs are rising
in areas such as technology, AI, compliance, as well as personnel.
Rising investment requirements mean that remaining subscale is
becoming more expensive, even for highly proﬁtable banks.

### Page 3

Domestic dominates, but cross-border
competes
UniCredit-Commerzbank remains the sector’s most closely
watched deal and could become the ﬁrst major cross-border
acquisition since the ﬁnancial crisis. UniCredit has slowly been
building its stake rather than a clean takeover and is now deemed
to hold eﬀective control, despite currently owning around 50% of
the shares. Furthermore, it is also not entirely a leap into an
unfamiliar market, as UniCredit already has a signiﬁcant banking

business in Germany through its subsidiary, HVB.
The deal has also faced considerable pushback as well as political
and stakeholder opposition, but is now looking increasingly likely to
proceed. It has highlighted the diﬃculty of pulling oﬀ a large-scale
cross-border deal for European banks. Yet it is now becoming the
clearest live test of whether Europe is genuinely ready for cross-
border bank consolidation.
Domestic deals remain the rule, but we have seen other examples

of cross-border deals. These include BPCE’s acquisition of
Portuguese peer Novo Banco; Erste Bank's acquisition of Santander
Bank Polska; and Crédit Agricole acquiring IKB to increase its
presence in Germany. Some banks are also looking across the
pond, such as Santander, which acquired Webster Financial
Corporation in the US for USD12.5bn.
These transactions are important, but they do not yet amount to a
broad-based cross-border consolidation wave. Many involve banks

already familiar with the target market or assets that complement
an existing local business.

Italy is still riding the wave
Italy has become one of Europe’s busiest M&A markets, riding a
new wave of consolidation, with Banca Monte dei Paschi (MPS) at
the epicentre. The bank completed the acquisition of Mediobanca in
2025, and this year approached Banco BPM and Banca Generali for
a merger as a counteroﬀensive to Intesa Sanpaolo’s hostile bid for
MPS.
The counteroﬀensive now appears less likely, as key shareholders

have opposed parts of MPS’s merger plans and have also backed
Intesa’s bid. The ECB has also provided authorisation to take control
of MPS, representing an important step for the €34bn takeover.
Alongside this, we had the BPER–Popolare di Sondrio merger earlier
in the year, adding to Italy’s consolidation.
Why is Italy so active in particular? Well, Italy remains quite
fragmented (see below) and still oﬀers a large domestic synergy
pool, even after the wave of M&A that has already taken place.

Furthermore, the country has meaningful industrial scale and oﬀers
further revenue diversiﬁcation opportunities for banks and insurers.
Although the obvious combinations are becoming less evident in
Italy, we expect this to continue to drive M&A activity in 2027.

It's not only about getting bigger
The deal mix is also broadening, as banks are increasingly focusing
on fee income as well as scale, while also stepping into capital-light
businesses. Deals in sectors such as wealth management, asset
management, payments & FX and insurance are likely to feature
prominently.
NatWest Group’s acquisition of Evelyn Partners is a good example.

The combination created the UK’s largest Private Banking and
Wealth Management business, while lifting Natwest’s fee income
by 20%. Similarly, BNP Paribas’s acquisition of AXA Investment
Managers in 2025 created a €1.5tn asset management business,
as the bank is seeking to compete with US asset management
rivals and grow its fee income. In Germany, Deutsche Bank and
DWS were reported to be exploring a potential minority investment
in the life insurance business Frankfurter Leben. This would mark

Deutsche’s return to life insurance after more than a decade.
Consolidation can support bank
performance
But does consolidation actually deliver better outcomes? The
evidence is not entirely clear-cut, but overall points in the right
direction. While increasing scale is not always the best use of
capital, in general, more consolidated markets tend to have more
stable and, in some cases, higher proﬁtability. The ECB is in favour
of further consolidation where appropriate, as it sees the beneﬁts of

capitalised banks tend to enjoy lower funding costs and thus have
more room to lend to the real economy.
The outcome therefore depends on the trade-oﬀ between longer-
term synergies and the upfront capital, execution and integration
costs.
ECB data show an association between more concentrated banking
markets and proﬁtability. As the chart below shows, countries with

a high level of concentration (deﬁned by a higher Herﬁndahl-
Hirschman Index coeﬃcient) tend to have higher net interest
margins on average. Still, net interest margins in Europe are
structurally lower than in the US, where they tend to be between 3–
4%.

Regulation is helping, but can get in the way
The Banking Union has made deals easier to assess: the ECB has
become the single prudential supervisor for large banks, capital
rules are more consistent, while resolution planning is now much

clearer than it was even a decade ago. But as the Draghi report
noted, the incomplete Banking Union means Europe’s banks remain
less proﬁtable than their US counterparts.
There is a shift underway, though. The EU Commission plans to
table a broad Banking Package in the ﬁrst quarter of 2027, with
measures covering cross-border capital and liquidity management,
deposit insurance, prudential rules and other items. For M&A, the
most important proposals include those to give banking groups

more ﬂexibility to allocate capital and liquidity more eﬃciently
within cross-border groups, while also applying more consistent

The AFME estimates that regulatory ring-fencing rules mean that
some €225bn of capital and €250bn of liquidity is trapped within
subsidiaries, which banks cannot freely move between parent and
subsidiaries. Freeing this up could also support more cross-border
transactions.
